# Scope of Work (extracted from Scope of Work (1).pdf)



<!-- Page 1 -->

             AIRPORTS AUTHORITY OF INDIA
                 RAJIV GANDHI BHAWAN,
                SAFDARJUNG AIRPORT

SCOPE OF WORK FOR DESIGN, DEVELOPMENT, IMPLEMENTATION
AND MAINTENANCE OF AIRSEWA AND PACR.

1.  Project Background

  Ministry of Civil Aviation had launched AirSewa web portal with mobile Apps on Android &
 IOS on 25th November 2016 as proof of concept. AirSewa provides a common platform to air
  travellers to submit grievances and seek flight/Airport information covering all stakeholders.
 The selected bidder should develop the AirSewa which shall be an advanced version of the
  existing portal and mobile application. AAI desires to improve upon the existing web portal
 &App and enhance the services. MoCA/AAI wants to develop AirSewa with addition of new
  features to current AirSewa application (AirSewa 1.0 features) as outlined in the below scope
  of work requirements. The focus of AirSewa would be to provide an upgraded platform to all
  stakeholders of civil aviation including air travellers to address various requirements and
  concerns of civil aviation passengers. AirSewa platform shall make good use of the latest
 SMAC (Social, Mobile, Analytics and Cloud) technologies, CRM technologies, location
  technologies, UI/UX technologies and other related technologies to provide a comprehensive
  digital platform to provide various desired features and functionalities needed for a delightful
  digital air travel experience.

2. Scope of Work

 The purpose of this section  is to define the scope of work of the bidder for design,
  implementation, and maintenance of the AirSewa Portal, Mobile Application and Passenger
  Assistance Control Room (PACR).

 The scope of the bidder primarily consists of the following four components:

   a)  Design and Development of AirSewa Grievance Redressal Platform: The bidder shall
      design, develop, operate, and maintain the AirSewa Platform, which will function as the
      core system for grievance redressal platform. The platform shall interact with users
      through a web portal, mobile application, and shall provide APIs to support integration
       with airlines, airport operators, and other required stakeholders.

     Operations and Maintenance of the AirSewa Platform: The bidder shall be responsible
      for the operation and maintenance of the AirSewa for a period of 3 years after the
     commissioning of the system which shall be extendable to 2 more years (total 5 years) on
      mutually agreeable terms. It would include application support and maintenance, database
     management, web-based SLA monitoring tool, backup and archival services etc. It shall
      also include administration, monitoring and management of system, storage and portal.



                                     Page 1 of 215

<!-- Page 2 -->

b)  Provisioning, Setup, Implementation – PACR

    Provisioning and deployment of complete call centre hardware and backend infrastructure
    in compliance with the prescribed specifications, including but not limited to desktops,
    headsets, telephony systems, and network components. Implementation and configuration
    of omni-channel contact centre solution with IVRS, including call flows, routing logic,
   dashboards, and reporting modules. API-based integration of all communication channels
   with AirSewa Agent Module for seamless ticketing and interaction handling. End-to-end
    setup covering testing, security compliance, go-live, and stabilization to ensure PACR is
    fully operational on a 24x7 basis and provide adequate manpower.

2.1. Project Objective


 The main objectives of the AirSewa platform and PACR are as follows:


2.1.1. Enhance efficiency of grievance redressal mechanism


 The primary purpose of the AirSewa platform is to provide passengers with a platform to
 escalate, submit, track, and resolve grievances related to air travel — including escalation
 regarding issues involving flights, refunds, baggage, airport facilities, and service quality in an
 easy and convenient way. Passengers can raise complaints digitally through the AirSewa web
 portal or mobile application, and these grievances would be routed to the concerned stakeholder
 for timely resolution.
                                    Page 4 of 215

<!-- Page 5 -->

2.1.2. Improve Accountability of stakeholders


    AirSewa strengthens accountability by ensuring that MOCA/AAI has  visibility into
    grievances of passengers or citizens against various aviation ecosystem stakeholders such
    as airlines, airport operators, and other stakeholders to ensure CAR compliance, response
     timelines, and quality of service delivery. The platform enables structured categorization
    of complaints (e.g., baggage, delays, ticketing, refunds), supports detailed reporting, and
    allows passengers to provide feedback on the resolution experience.

   Some of the complaints that can be registered on this portal are given below. The list is
     indicative and not exhaustive.

2.1.3. Enhance Aviation Governance Through Centralized Monitoring


    AirSewa will work as a central system that keeps track of:


               i.    Complaints by passengers towards stakeholders in aviation sector


               ii.    Quality of services provided


               iii.    Issues that need escalation and prioritization


 This  will  enable MOCA/AAI  to  maintain  an  oversight on  the  responses  and  the
 passengers/citizens will be able to directly reach out to MOCA/AAI for any grievance.

2.1.4. Make the grievance redressal system more comprehensive


 AirSewa aims to add options to address grievances of passengers through multiple modes of
 input.

2.1.5. Assure Superior Service Delivery & Performance Management:


                i.   SLA-Driven Performance: Embed Service Level Agreements (SLAs) directly into
             operational workflows, with automated monitoring, alerts, and escalations to ensure
            timely service delivery.

                ii.   Performance Analytics: Provide granular performance data for stakeholders/
          SPOCs/, departments, and specific service categories to identify rectification
           measures which could be related to policy, SOP, or training, etc.

                 iii.  Root Cause Analysis: Utilize collected data to identify systemic issues and
           implement long-term corrective and preventive actions, moving beyond reactive
            problem-solving.





                                    Page 6 of 215

<!-- Page 7 -->

 2.1.6. Maximize Operational Efficiency & Resource Optimization:


                 i.   Automation of Routine Tasks: Automate manual processes like coordination,
             tagging of complaints, etc to reduce the chances of human error, reduce response
            time and for optimum utilisation of human resources.

                  ii.   Real-time Resource Visibility: Provide real-time insights into complaints through
             dashboards, MIS system and reports.


 2.1.7. Cultivate Deep Citizen Engagement & Satisfaction:


         i.    Simplified Feedback Mechanisms: Offer diverse, user-friendly channels for citizens/
         passengers to provide feedback, suggestions, and report issues with minimal friction.

         ii.   Transparent Accountability: Ensure citizens have full visibility into the status and
         progress of their requests, fostering trust and demonstrating MoCA/AAI’s commitment
         to resolution.

         iii.  Reduce TAT of resolution with regular real-time updates on status of resolution, options
          for re-opening complaints, options to escalate, options to give feedback, etc.

2.1.8. Setup and operate Call Center for using Airsewa Agent module

           i.  To establish a centralized 24x7 Passenger Assistance and Grievance Redressal Centre
        (PACR) as a single point of contact for all stakeholder interactions.

           ii.  To enable seamless, omni-channel support integrated with AirSewa for efficient case
         management, tracking, and resolution.

           iii.  To ensure timely, transparent, and accountable grievance handling through defined
        SLAs, escalation mechanisms, and real-time monitoring.

        iv. To create a scalable, secure, and technology-driven support ecosystem capable of
         handling growing volumes and diverse stakeholder needs.

 2.3. Driving / Key Success factors for AirSewa

     Following are some of the key driving factors for the successful implementation and
     operation of AirSewa:

2.3.1. Strong backend mechanism

    One of the main objectives of the AirSewa is to enhance efficiency of grievance redressal
    mechanism in aviation landscape. To achieve this, the platform should have be easy to use,
    have quick TAT for resolution and strong backend mechanism for grievance redressal.

2.3.2. Ease of Integration with multiple stakeholders

    AirSewa is a grievance redressal portal in aviation sector which has multiple stakeholders.
     Thus, integration of the portal with other systems with these stakeholders is key success
     factor for quick resolution of grievances.

2.3.3. Protection of Information and Data Privacy
    AirSewa will have the data of users that raise ticket in the portal. Thus, the system
     should be compliant to Government of India’s policy on Data Privacy and Information
      security.

2.3.4. User Experience (Front end)

    To achieve high adoption and low bounce rate, AirSewa should provide superior user
     experience. Passenger/ CRO at PACR should find the process of raising and tracking
      ticket, creating a login, etc. very easy to use. There should not be any issues like filling long
     forms, login issues, OTP related issues, etc.

    2.4. Process Involved in AirSewa and PACR for Grievance Redressal





Figure - 2: AirSewa process for Grievance Redressal (Indicative)

The AirSewa portal is one of the grievance redressal channels amongst many. Other channels
can be social media, IVR, phone calls, voice mails, e-mails, etc. But AirSewa is also a case
management platform which shall monitor each ticket from start till complete closure. Even
the grievances received on other portals should be logged in the AirSewa portal for a unique id
generation or ticket number generation. To enable this platform, it will have separate landing
pages for the following type of users:

1.  Complainant: The complainants can be bonafide air passengers that might want to issue a
   complaint or share grievance against the concerned stakeholders using this app or through
    other channels.

2.  Redressor: The  departments  or  organisations  like  the  airlines,  airport  operators,
   government agencies like DGCA, etc are the ones that can redress the grievances mapped
    to them. They will need to access the AirSewa portal to address the complaints mapped to
   them and act as per the government guidelines.

   The representatives of Airline/Airport operators will also be seated at PACR premises to
    co-ordinate between the Call Center team and respective operators. They should also get
    ticket dashboards and can also comment on individual tickets if required.

                                   Page 15 of 215

<!-- Page 16 -->

 3.  Dashboard: MoCA/AAI and any other overarching agencies like DGCA, etc (with
    approval of MoCA) will monitor the status of overall complaints. They should be able to
    see a dashboard based on multiple parameters described in this RFP, extract reports, run
     queries, etc. to have an oversight over the grievance redressal process and intervene as and
   when required.

 The process of using AirSewa portal shall include the following stages:

2.4.1.   User raising complaint:

 To raise a complaint the user shall:

       i.    Register on the portal

      ii.   Login after registration

     iii.   Click on option to raise a new ticket

   iv.   Enter their details about:

              a. PNR

              b. Name

               c.  Phone number

              d.  Email id

              e.  Complaint type

                     f.   Brief description of issue

              g.  Upload evidence/ supporting information in photo/ video/ pdf/ word formats

     v.   Once the user submit the complaint, a case number/ ticket number is generated using
        which the user can track the following:
              a.  The agency it is assigned to,

              b.  The agency acknowledging it and working on it,

               c.  The agency diverting to another department/ stakeholder (in some cases  if
              needed)

              d.  The action taken by the agency/ stakeholder,

              e.  The expected TAT of resolution (based on the usual TAT of the officer/
              department looking into it),
                     f.  The ideal TAT for resolution for the type of complaint based on government
               guidelines
                                    Page 16 of 215

<!-- Page 17 -->

              g.  Cause of delay in resolution if any

              h.  The agency/ stakeholder shall message the user for any further information
                required.

         vi.   Closure of complaint

              a.  The user receives the information from the stakeholder about the action taken

              b.  The user may re-open the complaint ticket if not satisfied

               c.  The user may accept the resolution and give feedback

              d.  The user may initiate escalation in case of delay, urgency, etc.

2.4.2.   Redressor stakeholder addressing complaint

              i.   The  complaints  raised  by  the  user/  passenger  will  be  allocated  to  the
            stakeholder concerned depending on the type of issue.

              ii.   The stakeholder shall get an alert/ notification for every new complaint they are
           tagged to.

             iii.   The stakeholder shall verify within 1 hour if the complaint allocated to them is
            relevant or needs to be diverted to another stakeholder in case they are wrongly
         mapped or in case multiple stakeholder involvement is required.

         iv.   The stakeholder if verifies that the complaint is rightfully allocated, shall ask the
           concerned team to work on resolving the complaint immediately.

        v.    In case the complaint is wrongly tagged, they divert it to the concerned stakeholder/
          agency

         vi.   To resolve the complaint the stakeholder shall:

                   a.  Identify the problem

                   b.  Ask for additional information from the complainant if needed

                    c.  Verify if the complaint is genuine

                   d.  Check for the ideal resolution as per SOP and government guidelines

                   e.  Act as per guideline / SOP

                            f.  Keep the complainant posted/ informed through AirSewa about the action
                  being taken even when in process


                                    Page 17 of 215

<!-- Page 18 -->

2.4.3.   Verification:

              i.   The right closure of the complaint must be verified after the concerned agency
           claims to have resolved the complaint.
              ii.   The verification shall be AI based which will compare the resolution/ action
           taken to government rules/ guidelines:
             iii.   Feedback from complainant can help in ensuring the resolution was satisfactory

         iv.    In case the resolution was as per guidelines and the complainant still re-opens
           complaint or mentions that they are not satisfied with the resolution, manual
            intervention to check the issue with complainant will be required and suggested by
        SOP / AirSewa portal to the concerned stakeholder/ agency.

2.4.4.   Monitoring

              i.   The AirSewa will show the following information in the form of interactive
           dashboard and reports that can be extracted from the backend. Some of the
         commonly used information is given below and there may be more such analysis
          needed by MoCA/AAI.

                   a.  The complaints raised category wise like related to flight delays, flight
                    cancellation, baggage lost, baggage exchanged, food in flight, safety issue,
                    security issues, staff behavioural issues, etc
                   b.  The stakeholder wise complaints

2.5.1. User Personas

 The following section describes the personas of the various users that will be accessing the
 AirSewa platform. This is indicative and final user personas would be finalized with the bidder
 in discussion with MoCA/AAI
     1.  Complainants

        Passengers are the users logging complaints on the AirSewa portal from the complainant
         side.
     2.  Redressor

       These are staff / officers from the departments that will address the complaints logged on
        the AirSewa portal. Each department would be responsible for reviewing, owning,
        actioning and closing the complaints relevant to them. Indicative list of departments is as
        follows –

                      i.    Airline entities
                      ii.   Airport operator (AAI / Private agency)
                       iii.  OTA platforms for ticket booking like Make My Trip, Yatra, Goibibo, etc
                iv.  BCAS
              v.  DGCA
                vi.  CISF/ State police
     3.  Administration & Operations

       This persona includes users from MoCA/AAI and other overarching agencies like DGCA
      who would be responsible for monitoring the status of complaints, generate and view
        reports to evaluate the performance of the platform have an oversight over the grievance
        redressal process and intervene as and when required. These users should have access to
        dashboard, report generation system, run system queries, etc. for data-based decision
       making, pro-active interventions, predictive analysis and granular monitoring. The
        process flow explained in this document may sometimes be modified as per the suitability
        as decided by competent authority.
  2.5.2. User Interfaces

 This section elaborates the interfaces that the various user personas will interact with, and a high-
 level set of features provided by each interface.


 2.5.2.1. Complainant interface:

 This section elaborates the interfaces that should be available to the complainants for accessing
 and interacting with the AirSewa portal. The  list of interfaces to be made available to
 complainants is elaborated below –

                                   Page 21 of 215

<!-- Page 22 -->

    a) AirSewa mobile application in Android & iOS – Downloadable app from app stores
        that can be leveraged for raising and submitting a ticket, tracking its status and providing
       feedback.
    b) AirSewa web portal – Web portal accessible through all major internet browsers for
       accessing AirSewa
    c) AI Text Chatbot with Multilingual support – An AI text Chatbot that is integrated
       with the mobile app, web portal, WhatsApp and other social media channels. It should
       support 12 major Indian languages, excluding English and Hindi
    d) AI Voice Chatbot with Multilingual support – An AI voice Chatbot that is integrated
      and accessible Across the mobile app, web portal, and messenger app like WhatsApp. It
       should support 12 major Indian languages, excluding English
    e) Other channels – Apart from the above, complainants also register complains / express
       grievances Across various other channels. These complaints need to be documented and
        registered in the AirSewa portal in an automated manner, for further processing. These
       complaints would be reviewed and approved by the PACR team for further processing.
     An indicative list of other channels is below –
                    i.   WhatsApp/ Telegram
                    ii.   Phone call
                   iii.   Voice mail
             iv.   E-mail
            v.   IVR
             vi.   Complaints registered through Social Media platforms of  airlines, airports,
               regulators and the Ministry (Twitter, FB, Instagram, YouTube)
              vii.    Existing government public grievance platforms such as CPGRAMS, INGRAMS
             and others
              viii.    Physical letter

             ix.   Other communication channels as prescribed by MoCA/AAI in future

These modes should be digitized for input to the AirSewa portal using AI and other technologies,
as applicable. The final ticket to be logged to the AirSewa portal would be reviewed by PACR.
The tracking and case management of complaints raised on these modes shall be done using
AirSewa which will generate a unique id for each and identify redundant complaints, help in
tracking the cases end-to-end.

The workflows to be enabled by these interfaces is elaborated below -

2.5.2.2. User Registration:

           i.   The Passenger shall register on the portal/ app by giving details like name, phone
        number and e-mail id and username, etc. It should be enabled by factor authentication
        method and should be convenient for users at the same time. The information required
           to fill should be minimal to keep it short and convenient for User.
          ii.   Once authentication is successfully verified, the user is registered and can proceed to
          log in.
                                  Page 22 of 215

<!-- Page 23 -->

    2.5.2.3. Passenger login:

                  i.   The User login to be enabled by factor authentication method. Once verified the User
                 is logged in.
    2.5.2.4. Ticket Creation:

                  i.   The user enters minimal details like PNR (if applicable), grievance type, grievance
              description, Upload evidence/ supporting information in photo/ video/ pdf/ word
              formats. The form to be filled should be short, convenient and easy to understand. It
             should be multi-lingual and follow accessibility standards to make  it inclusive
             platform for users.
                   ii.   Tickets should be auto assigned to the concerned department on creation, based on the
              subject matter of the complaint / grievance
    2.5.2.5. Tracking of ticket till closure:

                  i.   Once the complaint is registered, the ticket number notification should be generated
            and sent to the User through SMS/ WhatsApp/ notification in app and web portal, etc.
                   ii.   The User should be able to track the complaints through app/ web portal and should
            keep getting updates on the status frequently through messages on phone like text
            message or message through messenger apps such as WhatsApp/ Telegram.
            Closure of Complaint: shall have the option to close the ticket on his/her own

                 i.   The User shall have the option to escalate the complaint at start or while it is in process.

                  ii.   The details of the escalated tickets will come to the PACR monitoring team. The team
               will follow up with the concerned stakeholders based on the ticket details.

    2.5.2.6. Re-opening ticket:

     Once the ticket is closed by the concerned stakeholder like airlines, airport operator, etc, the
      User shall have the option to:
                  i.   Re-open the ticket in case not satisfied

                   ii.   Escalate the complaint after re-opening

    2.5.2.7. Feedback:

      The User shall be asked for feedback at the time of closure of ticket/ complaint. The mode of
       asking for feedback can be voice call, message, form in the AirSewa portal, etc as decided
        in discussion with MoCA/AAI. The feedback form/ format should be short, easy to
       understand and convenient to fill.

2.5.5.     Business modules

   This section describes the backend business modules and application services that would be
   enabled by the AirSewa portal to meet the objectives outlined in this document.

2.5.6.     User Registration Module

    1.  Registration of user:

             i.   The user shall register on the portal/ app by giving details like name, phone number and
           e-mail id and username, etc. It should be enabled by factor authentication method and
          should be convenient for users at the same time. The information required to fill should be
         minimal to keep it short and convenient for User.
            ii.   Once the authentication is verified, User gets registered and can login.




                                     Page 27 of 215

<!-- Page 28 -->

    2.  User login:

             i.   The User login to be enabled by factor authentication method. Once verified, the User is
          logged in.
    3.  Guest login: the user should be enabled to login as a guest through factor authentication. The
       guest login shall give the option to user to give consent to save their data and create an
       account.
    4.  Access management: The nodal officers of organizations that are users should be able to
       control the access of staff and manage changes in the access to their staff in the portal.
    5.  Profile and preference management: the user can decide and opt for notification through
       various channels like email, SMS, notifications on app, etc.
    6.  Services tracking – Flight, Baggage, etc

             i.   The citizen should be able to put the flight number on the home page of portal without
          having to login the portal/ app
            ii.   The portal/ app should give the status and estimated time of arrival and landing at the
           correct airport to the user. For this the portal/ app should be able to fetch real-time data
           of flight status from the concerned stakeholder like airline, CATFM, etc, as decided by
       MoCA/AAI
           iii.   The user should be able to track a flight from AirSewa App/ portal by adding the flight
         number either on home page without logging in or after logging in.
        iv.   The system should allow the portal/ app to send regular updates through notifications,
        SMS, messenger app (Arratai/ WhatsApp/ Telegram, etc) message, email, etc to give real-
          time status of the flight in case the user opts for the same through option given in the
            portal
       v.   The portal shall allow tracking of components that are useful for passengers like flights,
          baggage, airport, etc as directed by MoCA/AAI
    7.  Crisis Notification: The portal should have the option of adding any notifications on home
      page as per directions by MoCA/AAI especially in case of major crises to caution the
       passengers/ users. For instance:
             i.   The flights are expected to delay in northern region due to fog for next 24 hours

            ii.   There is a crisis by ‘X’ Airlines due to technical glitch, likely to be resolved in next 2
           days, the
           users shall receive refunds for cancelled flights in next 5 business days.

   The AirSewa app/ portal will allow the users to raise and track the complaints through their login.

       of AirSewa portal.

      message through messenger apps such as WhatsApp/ Telegram.

     i.   The User shall have the option to close the ticket on his/her own

  Once the ticket is closed by the concerned stakeholder like airlines, airport operator, etc, the

2.5.8.     Redressor Module


    1.  The redressor like airlines, airport operators, DGCA, etc which shall be tagged to the
       complaints through AI based ticket allocation engine.

    2.  The ticket classification and allocation shall be based on parameters like key words, mapping
       of roles and responsibilities in the backend, and any other rules that are needed for correct
        auto-allocation in the system.

    3.  The redressor tagged shall also be allowed to divert to complaint to the other department/
       organisation or add more redressors in case needed.

    4.  In case of wrong tagging of the redressor, the AirSewa agents should be able to override the
       tagging and assign it to correct redressor.

    5.  The redressor should be able to communicate through the portal via comments/ messages/
       chat with the complainant, other officials and stakeholders mapped to the AirSewa portal

    6.  The tagged redressor should be able to add more stakeholders in case the ticket/ complaint
       required attention/ intervention from multiple stakeholders. The overall SLA for complaint
       resolution shall not be impacted if the additional redressor is added late, the timeline in which
      an additional redressor can be added shall be defined in the SOP.

    7.  The redressor should be able to compare the demand/ the action required to relevant
        guidelines/ SOPs to decide for the appropriate resolution. The AI tool shall assist in
      summarizing long complaint descriptions and in identifying the resolution suggestion for
        officer as per the DGCA or other relevant guidelines mapped in the system.

    8.  The redressor should be able to escalate the complaint to their seniors in case a decision is
      beyond their jurisdiction

    9.  The redressor should be supported by the system through AI based identification of duplicate
       grievances, however, this needs to be verified by the officials  / AirSewa agents if the
       duplicate identified by the AI tool is confirmed.

    10. The redressor should be able to close the complaint from their end once they resolve by
       uploading

           / adding evidence of relevant resolution such as the transaction id of the compensation of
        ticket transferred to the passenger (complainant)

    11. The redressor should be notified if the ticket is re-opened by the complainant

    12. Query management and dashboard for Redressor:

    iii.   They should be able to see their own performance in terms of their response time for

13. Tech help and feedback by redressor for portal

2.5.9.     Defined closure process


         1.  Stakeholders can close ticket, but passengers can reopen up to 2 times


         2.  Escalation triggers like escalation 1 resolution within 24 hours, escalation 2 after 72 hrs,
             etc. based on the benchmarks defined by MoCA/AAI and should be modified if the
         benchmarks are amended by MoCA/AAI from time to time. The escalation triggering
            shall be automatic in case:

                           i.   Of delays as per the guidelines set by MoCA/AAI/ DGCA.

                            ii.   Grievance case is urgent (like health emergency, medicines involved in the
                 baggage, admit card of a student in a baggage, etc.)

       Note: bidder shall define such priority cases to allow for prioritisation and escalation triggers
        for the same in discussion with MoCA/AAI and its stakeholders. There should be a provision
        to assign critical/ priority cases to PACR in the portal in case the SOPs suggest so.




                                     Page 32 of 215

<!-- Page 33 -->

         3.  Passenger feedback & verification post report closure:

           Ticket closure shall be permitted to the stakeholder responsible only once they share
         some evidence such as the transaction id of compensation paid, etc. The closure will be
          considered complete only if the passenger confirms the same in feedback

         4.  AI based closure verification based on CAR/ DGCA guidelines

         The resolution given/ action taken shall be verified by AI based mechanism at backend to
          check if the resolution is complaint to the government regulations/ guidelines which will
          have to be updated as per the amendments in the regulations/ guidelines from time to
           time.

         5.  Verification of resolution before closure:

         The following shall be the means to verify if the case can be considered closed:

                                   i.   Tagged airline/stakeholder must provide evidence before closure

                                   ii.   AI-based auto-verification aligned with CAR/DGCA rules

                                   iii.   Passenger feedback collected for every case

   The wrongfully closed or unverified closure cases shall be reinstated at any stage mentioned
   above. In case the passenger is not happy with the resolution while the AI based mechanism
    indicates the resolution was compliant to the guidelines, a manual intervention may be required
    to cross check the issue with the complainant. Such cases shall go to PACR for quick
    coordination and verification with various stakeholders, to take a call on the genuineness of the
    ask/ expectation by the Complainant.

2.5.10.    Redressor Admin Module

   The admin portal for each user organisation shall have a dashboard to monitor the workload, the
   TAT, quality of resolution, performance, etc. The portal should enable filtering and extraction
    of data in various reports to help monitor and act where needed based on data. The analytics that
   should be enabled in the portal shall include, but may not be limited to the following:

         1.  Operator performance analytics: the portal should allow the performance analysis of
         AirSewa Agents, operators in PACR, of officials handling the complaints for each user
           organisation, etc. The parameters may include Agent/queue load, average handling time,
            transfer rate, Quality scores from QA audits, coaching flags, etc.

         2.  Fairness checks for workload: the portal should enable Fairness checks for workload
            distribution/ allocation of tickets to operators: normalize by case complexity/severity,
         and suggested rebalancing of load.

         3.  Active v/s inactive queue: the portal should allow real-time monitoring of the movement
           of complaints from active to inactive queue and vice-a-versa.it shall include analysis of
          inflow vs. outflow, backlog, aging buckets, etc


                                     Page 33 of 215

<!-- Page 34 -->

         4.  Cross channel journeys: the portal should allow the monitoring of complainants moving
         from 1 channel to another to raise complaint such as a complainant may switch from web
         app to call for same complaint due to poor response time of dissatisfaction, etc.

         5. TAT analysis: the portal should allow analysis of TAT of resolution category wise, region
           wise, stakeholder, wise, etc

         6.  Resolution quality analysis: the system should allow monitoring of quality of resolution

         7.  The portal should allow governance check such as:

                  a.  Audit logs: to track configuration changes, role-based access events.

                  b.  Data retention checks

                   c.  Anomalies: sudden bulk edits, unusual login geos, high delete rates.

2.5.11.   AirSewa Agent Module

   AirSewa Agent Module shall be a part of the portal that enables the manual overriding of
   erroneous activities like ticket tagging, closure, verification of ticket closure, allocation, etc. The
    portal should allow the agents to do the following:

         1.  Overriding allocation and classification of complaints, in case the complaints are
         wrongly classified or wrongly tagged to a redressor by AI engine.

         2.  Reverification for closure: in case the ticket gets closed erroneously by the system

         3.  Compilation to avoid duplication of cases: in case there are multiple follow ups from
          complainant marked as a new complaint or any other cases causing duplication of
           complaints. There shall be provision to merge or link the follow up complaints to the
         main ticket id as per the SOP for such cases or as directed by MoCA/AAI.

         4.  Ticket allocation engine: the portal should allow allocation of tickets based on workload,
           expertise and other relevant parameters of the agents but if needed, the supervisor/ nodal
            officer may override that method of allocation and assign cases manually.

         5.  Query management: the agents should be able to filter complaints based on various
           parameters, extract reports, etc on need basis.

         6.  Communication channel for additional information: the portal should allow the agents to
         communicate with each  other, with complainant or redressor to seek or provide
           additional information.

         7.  Bulk case assessment and action: the portal should enable the agents to assess cases in
          bulk and take bulk action of Grievances such as override severity level, etc.

          going  to  rise during  certain  periods. This  will help  in deciding  the  additional
           headcount/shift adjustments effect on cost, contract and Service Level Agreement with

           provider need to act accordingly and patch the system. However, this will not be
          considered under the change request.

User Profiles & Account Management



                                  Page 40 of 215

<!-- Page 41 -->

   Access management provides the right for users to be able to use a service or group of services.
   Access management is the process of granting authorised users the right to use a service, while
    restricting access to non-authorised users. The system should be able to identify and authorize
    the users and would allow access to AirSewa portal and database. The user access management
   system would be able to identify the rights available with the user in terms of viewing, addition,
    deletion, modification of the data and generation of various reports through MIS.

   Content management system

   Content management system (CMS) is required to maintain the content on the AirSewa web-
    portal/ Mobile App. It should be possible to add content in form of Text, HTML, videos or
   Images on the department website. Along with the facility to easily publish content on the
    website, CMS would also have facility to maintain access logs for security and accountability
    purpose.

         a.  Configuration management and Version Control

             •  As the application undergoes enhancements and modifications due to problem
                requests, defect fixes and change requests, it becomes increasingly important to keep
               the  source code under  version  control and  the system under  configuration
             management.

             •  With the objective of keeping track of the version updates in the application, Bidder
                shall adhere to the configuration management process defined in conjunction with
           MoCA/AAI.

             •  Bidder shall ensure that a copy of the production environment is backed up and
               stored in the repository before the new  / modified components are copied to
               Production.

         b.  Release management

             •   Release management procedure shall be defined in conjunction with MoCA/AAI to
              ensure smooth transition of the application changes from release environment to
              production environment.

             •  As part of the release management Bidder shall perform the following activities:

             o   Bidders shall group the related change requests, assess their development
                   progress and accordingly prepare a schedule for their release.

             o   Bidder shall in consultation with MoCA/AAI prepare a detailed release plan for
                  every release. This plan shall include the release number and date of release. It
                     shall also contain details about the change request to be released.

1. AIRSEWA will provide an API ecosystem for integration with various stakeholder systems such
    as Airline, airport operators Online Travel Agents (OTAs) and other stakeholders mentioned in
    this document and figures above etc. to build innovative and meaningful commercial services.
   This must be built similarly to other Indian Digital Infrastructure projects like Digital Identity
    (Aadhaar), UPI etc. with the vision to create a vibrant eco-system around it.
2. MoCA/AAI  intends  to encourage  independent  application  developers  to  develop  tools
    (apps/portals) and other services for passengers relating to mentioned functions of AirSewa. To
    support this functionality, bidder shall publish and maintain the APIs, which will be consumed
   by these service providers. The information or query received through such apps/portals shall be
    received and processed by AIRSEWA.


3.  Following are some of the key principles for API framework:

          i.   API layer would not be exposed to untrusted connection.
         ii.   All external redressors who do not have their own CRM (e.g. All AAI airports) will
          connect to AIRSEWA through SSL Layer of authentication along with user id, single
           sign authentication and other important security features. Those who have CRM like
           Airlines the Call to action will be open in their CRM.

        iii.   All APIs level access to AIRSEWA (for users accessing the system) should be through
       HTTPS

      iv.  AIRSEWA should have provision to support issuance of license key / sub licenses key
           including validation of the same in the system.

      v.   All data transfer from / to AIRSEWA to happen through APIs
      vi.  App signature authentication will be through the license key + time stamp + app version
         and other meta data

      vii.   All the APIs would be stateless in nature, thus easy to load balance, even if hit through
            portal is very high and this requires high end processing.





                                     Page 44 of 215

<!-- Page 45 -->

      viii.  MoCA/AAI would prescribe the mechanism for empanelment of third-party systems who
            will use the AIRSEWA APIs and build apps using the same

      ix.   Bidder would deploy a developer sandbox to test the APIs with dummy data.
      x.  An API design document with the specification to start developing the interfaces. The
         APIs may be RESTful services with XML payload and may have the following minimum
           information in the design document but not limited to

         a.  Purpose of API
         b.  Author & Owner of API (controlling entity)
         c.  Input parameters
         d.  Output
         e.  Error codes


4. AIRSEWA is envisaged to be an API based solution where external agencies / eco system partners
    will also build & manage APIs as well as will set up secured networks to access the AIRSEWA.
   Hence, it is important that the bidder sets up, manages and monitors the API services for
   AIRSEWA. This entire framework needs to be set up and operationalised as part of the scope of
   work. The broad scope of the bidder relating to this function is detailed below:

          i.   Enrolment and operations
       MoCA/AAI will be the overall regulator and overseer of the API based system. As part of
            this project, bidder will set up the requisite process as well as system to build, operate &
         manage and sustain APIs for third party service provider in a secured and controlled
          environment. This will establish secure connectivity compliant with AIRSEWA standards
         and specifications. Third party service provider will offer their AIRSEWA-compliant
          network connectivity as a service and transmit authentication requests to AIRSEWA.
          Third Party Systems will use AIRSEWA authentication to enable  its services and
          connects to the AIRSEWA through an internet connectivity after validation of license
           key.
         ii.   Authorization and License Key Management
          Bidder should create an administrative portal to enable third party systems to have a user
          account to manage their services. System shall ensure only the authorized persons of
           third-party system have access to this admin portal.
        iii.   Standardizing API and specification
         Based on the solution developed API needs to be standardized and specification must be
          defined so that it can be exposed in the stakeholder ecosystem and third-party systems
          can create their own services and expose them to the outer world for stakeholder use.
          Bidder should have the ability to define, document, edit and publish API specifications.


      iv.   Environment Management
                                     Page 45 of 215

<!-- Page 46 -->

      Bidder should provide a sandbox environment where third party systems can publish a
     mock version of APIs. The admin portal shall be used to create third party systems Dev
      IDs  that can be accessed by the developers of  potential  third-party systems  for
      development, test and integration. User can perform testing in a sandbox environment
      which is distinct from production. Sandbox shall provide the same catalogue as the
       production framework. All the APIs shall be hosted in sand box environment to ensure
        at-least couple of  third-party systems  integrate/test before the API  is moved  to
       production.
  v.   User Authentication
      Bidder to provide authentication services for allowing users to access the above-
      mentioned environments and to do the following operations
            a) To authenticate user into the Sandbox.

            b) To configure authorisation policy for new APIs as they are introduced to the
             framework.

             c)  Allow user to access the available APIs and associated properties in accordance
             with his/her entitlements.
            d)  Allow third party app exposed to the API, resources data in accordance with
              the configuration for that app.
            e)  Blacklist/Block Access

 vi.   Publishing and Management of API
      There should be a mechanism that allows authorized users to publish new APIs as they
       are created to sandbox, test and production environments as required. Once the API are
      developed and deployed in the sandbox environment bidder to do a proper functional,
       security and performance test and certify the API before being published for production
       usages. An API catalogue should be maintained by the system.
 vii.   Version Control
      Bidder should provide a controlled mechanism for API versioning control for any
      change. The version & release management process must cover this aspect to ensure any
      change is made or rolled out in a controlled & informed manner.
viii.  API Retirement
      Bidder should provide a mechanism to retire/archive APIs. The solution should provide
        full support of managing, retiring and archiving APIs as part of the life cycle and
       associated version control.
 ix.  API Governance & SLA enforcement





                                  Page 46 of 215

<!-- Page 47 -->

     Bidder should provide a mechanism to define and enforce SLAs/quotas for consuming
       entities of the API framework. The solution should provide “Plan” such as to control how
    much traffic can be sent by each user through the interface. A Plan to make available a
      collection of resources from one or more APIs, or a plan defines a rate limiting policy
      that specifies how many requests an application is allowed to make during a specified
     time interval, and what action should be taken when the threshold is exceeded. The
      solution should support both a hard limit which throttles the traffic and a soft limit which
      notifies the administrator about the policy violation. For soft limit the solution should be
      further customised to buffer the extra messages to shape the  traffic. Application
      developers gain access to APIs by registering their applications to access plans by using
      the developer portal.
      Solution should also provide real time SLA monitoring capability. Solution should enable
      configuration of system SLAs. The APIs load shall be continuously and pro-actively
     monitored  for  suitable & prompt actions  in case of excessive loads,  failures or
     performance bottlenecks. Administrator should perform appropriate actions as SLA
      thresholds are approached/ reached
       - including notifications, throttling, rejection of requests, raising alarms.

    API framework and AIRSEWA must work together to synchronize API and back-end
      service policies. Additionally, API lifecycle stages should be synchronized with system
      service  implementation  stages. An API Governance  experience may  provide  a
      straightforward set of lifecycle stages (e.g., created, published, deprecated, retired,
     blocked) that may be customized by the development team.
 x.   API Updates, Notification and tech support


     Bidder should provide a mechanism to provide consuming entities with appropriate
      notifications with respect to APIs. The solution should ensure that application developers
    who wish to access and use the APIs are self-sufficient. Documentation about an API, such
      as URL used to call the API and the security mechanism used by the API to authenticate
      application user, is automatically generated when defining the API and exposed through
      the  developer  portal.  Additional supporting documentation  that can  further help
      application developer to use the API, such as samples and/or tutorials and other supporting
     documentation shall be available through the developer portal.
xi.  API Security Governance
      Solution should provide appropriate & adequate security mechanisms governing access
      to API framework- i.e. Authentication strength. The system should inspect the headers
      for APIs genuineness before acceptance. It should apply all security checks e.g. DDoS
      Attacks, XML Denial of Service (xDoS), Slow down or disable an XML based System,
     Message Snooping, XML Document Size Attacks, XML Document Width Attacks,
   XML Document Depth Attacks, Jumbo Payloads, Recursive Elements, Public Key DoS,
   XML Flood, Resource Hijack  etc. to ensure  rightful and secured access to API
     consumers.




                                 Page 47 of 215

<!-- Page 48 -->

          Solution should allow the audit tracking of dev/client apps consuming APIs and portal

                a)  User ID/Credential
                b)  Transaction data
                 c)  IP addresses of source and destination

                e)  Session duration
                        f)  Session  ID/key  (Session key must be  dynamically  generated  for  every
                   transaction session key must not be reused)

     xiii.  API Usage Activity

        The prioritisation optimisation refers to the service that supports in managing workload

        The system should allow nodal officers to create more logins, manage access to various

             iii.   Verification process of complaint closure

         The following shall be the means to verify if the case can be considered closed:
                                     Page 48 of 215

<!-- Page 49 -->

              a.  Tagged airline/stakeholder must provide evidence before closure

              b.  AI-based auto-verification aligned with CAR/DGCA rules

               c.  Passenger feedback collected for every case

         The closure will be considered complete only if the passenger confirms the same in
           feedback. In case the passenger is not happy with the resolution while the AI based
         mechanism  indicates  the  resolution was  compliant  to  the  guidelines, a manual
           intervention may be required to cross check the issue with the complainant. Such cases
            shall go to a Case Closure Committee to take a call on the genuineness of the ask/
           expectation by the Complainant. This Committee shall be chaired by the representative
         from MoCA/AAI/ DGCA and will decide the optimum resolution or may decide to close
           the case/ ticket if it is found to be ingenuine.
2.5.25.    Indicative Illustration of Use Cases PACR–Stakeholder Integration
         These illustrative use cases demonstrate the intended operating model of PACR as a
           centralized coordination and monitoring platform, enabling passengers  to receive
           consistent updates and enabling AAI to oversee stakeholder responsiveness through an
           integrated omni-channel CRM, Chatbot and Helpdesk system.
2.5.26.   Refund Request for Cancelled Flight Ticket / Delay in Refund

          1. A passenger raises a request through PACR regarding non-receipt or delay in refund
             following cancellation of a flight ticket.
          2.  The PACR CRM registers the request and identifies the concerned airline based on
               flight and booking details.
          3.  The PACR system triggers a call to action in the airline’s CRM/service management
             system, transmitting passenger  details, booking reference, refund category, and
              timelines.

          4.  The airline validates the request and updates its system with one of the following
              actions:
              •  Refund under process with tentative amount and expected date
                  Refund rejected with reason
                  Refund completed with transaction reference and disbursement date
          5.  Each status update is synchronized back to the PACR system and automatically
           communicated  to  the  passenger  through  configured  channels  (email/ SMS/
            WhatsApp/portal/IVR).

          6.  On completion of refund or final rejection, the airline closes the case in its system,
           which triggers corresponding closure in the PACR system.



                                     Page 49 of 215

<!-- Page 50 -->

   Workﬂow: Refund Request for Cancelled
     Flight





                                                             Auto-Close: Case closure in airline system
                                                                 automatically closes PACR ticket



2.5.27.    Missing / Lost / Left-Behind Baggage – Return or Refund

          1. A passenger approaches PACR regarding missing,  lost, or left-behind baggage,
             seeking tracking, return, or compensation/refund.
          2.  The PACR CRM logs the case and routes  it to the responsible airline or ground
             handling agency based on baggage tag and flight details.
          3.  The PACR system triggers a call to action in the stakeholder’s system with baggage
             reference details and passenger contact information.
          4.  The stakeholder updates its system as baggage tracing progresses, including:
                  Baggage located and return initiated
                  Compensation/refund process initiated
                  Request closed with reason (e.g., baggage recovered and delivered)
          5.  Status updates, including tentative timelines and final outcomes, are synchronized to
              the PACR system and communicated to the passenger.
          6.  Closure or reopening of the case in the stakeholder’s system is mirrored automatically
              in the PACR system.

2.5.28.    Airport Amenities Issue or Complaint against Airport Operators

          1. A passenger submits a complaint, issue, or suggestion through PACR regarding airport
             amenities such as cleanliness, signage, seating, washrooms, accessibility, or facilities
              for senior citizens and persons with reduced mobility.

          2.  The PACR CRM registers the case and assigns it to the concerned airport authority or
               airport operator based on location and issue category.

          3. A call to action is triggered in the airport authority’s/operator service or facility
           management system.

          4.  The airport authority/operator records actions taken, such as:

                  Issue acknowledged and rectification initiated

                  Corrective action completed

                  Suggestion reviewed and accepted/noted

          5.  The status of corrective action and closure remarks are synchronized back to the PACR
             system.

          6.  The passenger receives updates and closure confirmation through PACR channels.

2.5.29.    Delay or Non-Response by Stakeholder Beyond SLA


          1. A passenger’s case assigned through PACR remains unresolved beyond the defined
          TAT/SLA.

          2.  The PACR system automatically flags the breach and triggers:

                  Automated reminders to the concerned stakeholder

                  Escalation notifications to designated supervisory levels

          3.  Continued non-compliance results in:

                  Mass or case-specific notices/memoranda generated through PACR

                   Visibility of SLA breaches on AAI dashboards

          4.  Resolution and subsequent closure updates are synchronized Across systems once
              action is taken.

2.5.30.   System Expectations Across All Use Cases


       1.  Across all scenarios, the PACR system shall ensure:

                Bi-directional, real-time or near real-time integration with stakeholder systems

               Unified ticketing with synchronized status, closure, and reopening

               Omni-channel passenger communication independent of ticket origination channel

               Complete audit trail of all actions, communications, and timelines

               Consolidated dashboards for AAI to monitor stakeholder performance and passenger
              experience

       2.  These illustrative use cases demonstrate the intended operating model of PACR as a
           centralized coordination and monitoring platform, enabling passengers  to  receive
           consistent updates and enabling AAI to oversee stakeholder responsiveness through an
           integrated omni-channel CRM and Helpdesk system.

2.5.31.    PACR Integration for any stakeholder without Native CRM Systems  (e.g.
    Airport Operators, CISF, BCAS etc)

       1.  Background: Certain airport operators manage multiple airports but do not maintain a
          centralized digital CRM or grievance management system, relying instead on landline-
         based helpdesks and manual registers. This annexure illustrates how such operators shall
         be enabled to participate in the Passenger Assistance and Control Room (ACR) ecosystem.

       3.   Illustrative Stakeholder: “AAI” – an airport operator managing multiple airports,
          without an existing CRM system.

       4.  Objective: To enable airport operators without native CRM systems to digitally receive,
         manage, update, and resolve passenger grievances through the PACR CRM platform, Use
         Case Description

               Role-Based Access: PACR shall provide role-based login access to the airport
               operator for each airport managed by it. Designated Helpdesk Head(s) / authorized
             team members shall be mapped to their respective airports, with access restricted to
               relevant tickets only.

               Passenger-Initiated Grievance via PACR: Passengers may register grievances
                related to the operator’s airports through any PACR omni-channel (call centre,
             AirSewa, email, social media, etc.). PACR shall register the ticket, categorize the
                issue, identify the responsible airport, and generate a call-to-action visible in the
               respective airport’s PACR login.

               Ticket Handling by Airport Helpdesk: The airport helpdesk shall acknowledge,
               update, and resolve the ticket directly within PACR. All actions and communications
                shall be time-stamped and tracked against applicable SLA/TAT norms.

               Stakeholder-Initiated Ticket Creation: The airport helpdesk may also create tickets
                directly in PACR for walk-in complaints, landline calls, or operational observations.
            Such tickets shall follow the same lifecycle, escalation, and audit mechanisms.

               Ticket Number Mapping and Visibility: PACR shall allow tagging and mapping
               of ticket numbers generated by the airport operator, airlines, or other stakeholders to
             PACR-created tickets. PACR shall be able to view the complete ticket history and
              maintain synchronized references for traceability.

               Monitoring, Escalation, and Oversight: PACR dashboards shall provide real-time
                 visibility to AAI and authorized users on grievance volumes, resolution timelines,
             and SLA compliance Across all airports of the operator. Automated reminders,
                escalations, and a complete audit trail shall support regulatory and administrative
                oversight.

       5.  ensuring end-to-end visibility, SLA compliance, auditability, and effective coordination
          with AAI and other stakeholders.

   Data Layer consist of Databases mapped to the technical requirements of the platform. An

   The portal should allow chat, e-mail and notifications, comments by stakeholders that are

    coordinate manually.
            i.   Simple form requiring only PNR, grievance type, description, and evidence

             iii.  Option to reopen tickets

         iv.   Multilingual interface for wider accessibility.

   The AirSewa shall be the core of the system. It will host various passenger services as detailed

2.5.34.    Chatbot Support

   The features of the Chatbot will be as follows:
                                     Page 55 of 215

<!-- Page 56 -->

         1.  Responding to  all the passenger queries related to  registration,  creation,  etc. on
       AIRSEWA website/ mobile application. If the passenger is not satisfied, Chatbot solution
          should have the capability to forward the query to help desk official and update the status
           of query to customer after its resolution.
         2.  Leveraging technologies like Natural Learning Process (NLP) and Artificial Intelligence
           (AI) to enhance contextual understanding, generative responses and learning abilities
          while doing end to end conversation with passengers.
         3.  Providing an analytical tool which can analyse the passenger data created during
           conversations based on various parameters like sentimental analysis, etc.
         4.  Interacting with the user and responding to various level queries raised in English.

         5.  Understanding & executing compound query, at the same time ability to identify different
            intents and act upon both simultaneously.
         6.  Not storing any of the information entered by user anywhere locally on device and it
          should be stored in encrypted format on servers only.

2.5.35.   AI Automation suite

   SI shall be responsible for automation of the following processes by use of advance AI
   mechanism:
         1.  Auto tagging and AI driven verification to reduce manual effort:
                    i.   The bidder shall be responsible for creating an exhaustive list of complaints that can
                 arise, decide relevant bucketing of those into categories. This can be modified and
             updated to improve the efficiency of the system.
                    ii.   The system should be able to pick up the key words from the complaint details given
            by the User, map it to correct category and tag it to the relevant stakeholder/ authority/
              department.
                   iii.   The system should be able to verify the resolution by comparing it with the relevant
               guidelines mapped in the system. These guidelines and regulations like Civil
              Aviation Regulations (CAR) and other such guidelines should be updated in real time
                 at backend to avoid the wrong verification and closure of reports leading to multiple
              re-opening and escalations of the complaints.
         2.  Automatic routing of grievances to the relevant team/SPOC
         3.  Auto triggered escalation workflow especially in priority cases

                    i.   The bidder shall define an exhaustive list of cases where the grievance case is urgent
                (like health emergency, medicines involved in the baggage, admit card of a student
               in a baggage, etc.) and should be put on priority. This list should be signed off by
          MoCA/AAI and added to the backend. This list should be updated from time to time
            by learning from past cases if any modifications are required.
                    ii.   Based on the defined cases that need to be taken on priority, the system should be able
               to recognise such cases based on the key words used by complainant and should be
               highlighted as a priority case to the stakeholder tagged to the complaint. The TAT of
               resolution of such cases shall be lesser as defined by bidder after discussion and sign-
               off from MoCA/AAI.
         4.  Predictive congestion reporting for early issue detection:

         The system shall be able to predict congestion reporting based on data and past trends.
         The probable causes of congestion could be:
                                     Page 56 of 215

<!-- Page 57 -->

                     i.   Poor performance of the airlines like multiple flight delays and cancellations

                     ii.   Change in some regulations by government

                    iii.   Seasonal issues like delays/ cancellations/ diversions due to fog

              iv.   High volume of traffic expected during special events in a city

             v.    Hostile / war like situation
              vi.   Airspace closure

               vii.    Airport/ runway closure due to technical issues like failure in equipment functioning
            on runway, etc.
     To predict the same, relevant data needs to be collected/ fetched by the system from relevant
       stakeholders in the aviation sector. This will require multiple integrations which have been
       described in subsequent sections.
         5.  AI assistance in summarising grievance and mapping it to relevant rules for the Redressor
                / MoCA/AAI, to help decide the right redressal of the grievance
         6.  Conversational AI to translate speech to text for multiple languages for the grievances
           received.

         7.  AI based ticket classification and allocation to the concerned redressor/ stakeholder

         8.  AI based identification of duplicate cases.

2.5.36.   Cloud and PACR Infrastructure

           2.5.36.1 Overall Cloud requirements:

                        i.  Cloud Service Provider (CSP) must be Meghraj 2.0 as managed by NIC/NICSI, for
               the relevant deployment model and services with ongoing STQC audit as per the
            MeitY empanelment process.

                         ii. CSP must comply with all security-related requirements, guidelines, and regulations
               issued by MeitY, CERT-In, RBI, Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) or any standards
            body recognized by the Government of India. This includes existing and future
             mandatory standards that Government of India may notify from time to time.

                          iii. The CSP shall provide the required cloud services from its Data Centre facilities
               located within India.

                  iv. The primary and DR Data Centre must be in separate seismic zones.

                v. The CSP shall ensure that underlying Data Centre facility must be at least Tier III or
              higher standard (certified under TIA 942 or Uptime Institute certifications) and
             implement tool-based processes based on ITIL standards.

                  vi. CSP shall also ensure that all the purchaser data and system logs shall be stored
             and processed within India in the MeitY-empanelled regions/facilities.

        vii. Vulnerability assessment shall be conducted each quarter and to be rectified by SI &
        yearly penetration testing for complete applications and platform, the compliance
        report needs to be submitted to MoCA/AAI.

2.5.36.2 Cloud and PACR service requirements:

     1.  The cloud services must provide scalable, redundant and elastic compute and storage
        resources.

     2.  The service must allow configuration of VMs with required vCPU, RAM, and disk
        capacities.

     3.  The service must allow users to procure and access compute and storage capabilities
       remotely over SSL with two-factor authentication.

     4.  The service must support VM image backups and enable taking, importing, and
       exporting of VM instances in a MeitY approved image format.

     5.  Configuration and management of  virtual machines must be accessible only
       through SSL-based VPN clients and not via the public internet.

     6. SP Shall ensure that all VMs receive operating system patching, health checks,
       systematic attack detection, and backup functions.

     7.  The service must monitor VM availability and resource utilization, including RAM,
      CPU, disk, IOPS, and network metrics.

     8.  The purchaser shall retain the complete ownership of all virtual machines, templates,
        clones, and scripts/applications created for the organization.

     9.  The purchaser shall have the right to request full copies of any virtual machine at any
        time.

    10. The purchaser shall retain the ownership of all business applications, solutions, or
       bespoke software installed on VMs.

    11. The service must support secure administration interfaces (e.g., SSL/TLS or SSH)
        for authorized personnel to remotely manage their virtual instances.

    12. The service must support dynamic allocation of virtual machines based on load,
       without service interruption.

    13. The service must provide the capability to copy or clone VMs for archiving, testing,
       and troubleshooting.

    14. The SP shall provide secure, encrypted tools and mechanisms for the purchaser or its
       appointed agency to define backup requirements and policies, as well as to configure,
        schedule, perform, and manage backups and restores for all data types including files,
        folders, images, system states, databases, enterprise applications etc.

    15. The SP shall support data transfer back to the purchaser’s environment either on
      demand or upon termination of the contract.
                               Page 58 of 215

<!-- Page 59 -->

    16. The SP shall not delete any purchaser data for a period of up to 90 days after contract
       expiry unless expressly approved by the purchaser.
    17. The cloud platform must support live migration of running VMs from one host to
        another.
    18. The service must support all APIs provided by the SP, including REST APIs.
    19. The SP must offer fine-grained access controls including role-based access control
      (RBAC), SSL certificates and multi-factor authentication.
    20. The SP  shall ensure  that  offered cloud platform and  services must support
       comprehensive auditing, including details of requests made, source IP, user identity,
       timestamp, and related events.
    21. The SP shall ensure that the purchaser shall be permitted to bring, upload and run
       properly licensed non-OS software required for their solutions by using it directly on
      VMs.
    22. VM CPU and RAM must  automatically  scale during load spikes  to ensure
        application availability.
    23. Native cloud-based storage solutions may be used if they meet all stated requirements
       and are supported by appropriate specifications and customer references.

     1.  Manage  the  network,  storage,  server  and  virtualization  layers,  to  include

          email address five (5) days prior to patch cut-off dates

2.5.36.5 Infrastructure provisioning for cloud and PACR requirements:

The objective of this RFP is to select a Service Provider (SP) who shall be responsible for
Design, Development, Integration, Deployment and Maintenance of Grievance Management
System (GMS), PACR and associated Customer Relationship Module (CRM) on Cloud
Infrastructure.

2.5.36.6 Cloud Deployment Model

    i.   The entire GMS and CRM solution shall be deployed on a Virtual Private Cloud
     (VPC)– based infrastructure.

    ii.   All monitoring, provisioning, should be within India and 100% isolated from other
      regions outside India, if in case CSP has Global presence, on Data Centres empanelled
      with MeitY.

2.5.36.7 Primary & Disaster Recovery (DR) on cloud Environment Setup

    i.   The solution must be deployed on Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)–based infrastructure
      and Disaster Recovery (DR- Cloud Based) sites.

    ii.   The infrastructure shall support automated DR (cloud based), ensuring seamless
       continuity of business operations with no single point of failure.

2.5.36.8 Scalability & Performance

    i.   The offered cloud platform and PACR Infra must provide inherent auto-scaling
       capabilities, enabling vertical and horizontal scaling based on workload.
    ii.   The system should sustain peak load conditions without performance degradation
      or service disruption. The additional resources to be reduced when the load is reduced
       (off peak phases).

2.5.36.9 Infrastructure Provisioning

    i.   The cloud environment shall include all required:

      o  Compute resources (servers, VMs, containers)
      o  Storage components (block, object, archival)
      o  Networking components (router, switches,)
      o  Cybersecurity components (VPC, firewalls, load balancers, VPN, connectivity to
          cloud hosting environment)
    ii.   Infrastructure must be equipped with secure, redundant, and high-throughput
      connectivity for the cloud hosting environment.

    2.5.37.8.  Objective and Governance

   The Bidder shall design, implement, operate, and continuously improve a cybersecurity and
    privacy-by-design framework for AirSewa and the integrated PACR environment to prevent,
    detect, respond to, and recover from cyber threats and risks. The framework shall align with the
    overall solution landscape given in this RFP and ensure secure-by-default deployments Across
    applications, APIs, data stores, analytics, network, endpoints, and cloud services.
   The Bidder shall provide timely and standardized sharing of threat, vulnerability, and incident
   information with MoCA/AAI/AirSewa and PACR and ensure continuous compliance with
    applicable Government of India policies, laws, and guidelines.
 2.6. Roles and Responsibilities of the Bidder

   The Bidder’s scope includes, but is not limited to, the following. All activities shall be performed

          changes/incidents.

      i.     Detect,  log,  classify, and  report  security  events/incidents; coordinate containment,
