2.6.14.    Security Architecture and Controls

2.6.15.   Network Security (AirSewa + PACR)
2.6.16.   Zero Trust & Segmentation
            o  Implement layered network segmentation (VPC/VNet, subnets, VLANs, security
                groups/NSGs, micro-segmentation) Across application tiers, databases, analytics,
                and management planes.
            o  Enforce least-privilege east-west access; deny-by-default policies; explicit egress
                    controls.
2.6.17.    Secure Connectivity & Admin Access
            o  All admin access via SSL/TLS VPN or bastion hosts with MFA; no direct public
                  administration endpoints.
            o  DNS security, NTP time sync (per CERT-In), and secure management channels
               (SSH, HTTPS) only.
2.6.18.    Perimeter & Ingress Controls
            o  Next-Gen Firewall (NGFW) with IPS/IDS at ingress/egress; WAF in front of
                  public-facing apps and API Gateway with OWASP Top-10 protections.
            o  DDoS protection, bot/abuse controls, geo/ASN filtering where applicable.
2.6.19.   API Security
            o  API Gateway enforces authentication, authorization, schema validation, rate
                    limiting, quotas,  throttling, anomaly detection, signature/timestamp checks;
               mutual-TLS for trusted backends.

                                     Page 70 of 215

<!-- Page 71 -->

2.6.20.   Network Detection & Response
            o  NIDS/NDR  telemetry  integrated  to SIEM,  alerting on  lateral movement,
                    exfiltration patterns, suspicious flows.
2.6.21.    Email & Messaging Security
             o  SPF/DKIM/DMARC for email; secure SMTP relays; link/file sandboxing for
                    high-risk channels (email/social/WhatsApp ingest pipelines).
2.6.22.    Cybersecurity Controls (Applications, APIs, Endpoints, Containers)
2.6.23.    Identity & Access Management
            o  SSO, MFA for privileged/sensitive operations; RBAC/ABAC, SoD, PAM for
                 admin/service accounts; SCIM/JIT provisioning; IP allowlists for admin.
            o   PKI/digital signatures and non-repudiation  for documents/transactions where
                   required.
2.6.24.    Authentication & Passwords
            o  Unique IDs, strong password/passphrase policy (breached/common password
                    blocklists), rotation and reuse prevention per policy; device/biometric options
               where applicable.
2.6.25.    Secure SDLC & AppSec
            o  Align with OWASP ASVS and OWASP Top-10; implement SAST/DAST/SCA,
                   secrets scanning, dependency pinning, SBOMs; code signing; IaC scanning for
                 cloud resources.
            o  Security gates in CI/CD; pre-production VAPT and remediation prior to go-live.
2.6.26.    Endpoint & Workload Protection
            o  EDR/EPP on servers/endpoints/containers; allow-listing where feasible; kernel-
                   level anti-malware; quarantine/remediation orchestration via SOAR.
            o  Container/Kubernetes: image signing, private  registries, admission controls,
                 runtime policy enforcement, CIS benchmarks.
2.6.27.   Data Protection & Privacy
            o  Encryption in transit (TLS 1.2/1.3) and at rest (KMS/HSM); secrets management;
                    field-level masking and format-preserving encryption where feasible.
            o  Information asset classification; purpose limitation; consent capture and data
                 minimization; privacy-by-design for analytics/training pipelines.
2.6.28.    Operational Security
            o  Centralized SIEM with correlation rules/use-cases; SOAR playbooks for P1–P3
                   scenarios; red/blue/purple exercises as scheduled.
            o  DLP where applicable  (e.g., export/download from portals/BI  tools), with
                watermarking and access governance.
            o
                                     Page 71 of 215

2.6.29.    Security for Analytics & BI Tools (Power BI, Tableau, etc.)
2.6.30.    Access & Identity
            o  SSO (SAML/OIDC), MFA  for  admins/publishers;  service  principals  for
                 automation; disable legacy auth.
2.6.31.    Content Governance
            o  Workspace/Site   strategy  with  environment  separation  (Dev/Test/Prod);
                  Project/Folder permissions, peer review, and content promotion workflows;
                ownership and stewardship defined.
2.6.32.   Data Security
            o  Row-Level Security (RLS) and Object-Level Security (OLS)  for  datasets;
                 parameterized filters; data lineage and impact analysis enabled.
            o  Governed gateways (on-prem/cloud) for data refresh; credential vaulting; keys
               managed via KMS/HSM; dataset/extract encryption at rest.
2.6.33.    Publishing & Sharing Controls
            o   Restrict public sharing and “publish to web”; allow only named users/groups;
                time-boxed access; watermark sensitive visuals; export/download controls and
            DLP rules for CSV/PDF/images.
2.6.34.    Audit & Monitoring
            o  Enable Power BI audit logs / Tableau Server logs and admin views; integrate with
             SIEM (access, dataset refresh failures, abnormal exports, mass access attempts).
2.6.35.    Platform Hardening
            o  Harden Power BI tenants, Tableau Server/Cloud (TLS, cipher suites, admin roles,
                  backup/restore encryption, secrets rotation); VAPT on embedded analytics flows;
                keep within India data residency constraints where applicable.
2.6.36.    Database Security

    2.6.36.1 Architecture & Access
            o  Dedicated subnets; database firewall/NSGs; private endpoints; no public exposure.
            o  RBAC/ABAC for DB roles; least privilege; PAM for DBA access; break-glass
                 accounts with approvals and full audit.
2.6.37.    Encryption & Keys
            o  Encryption at rest (e.g., TDE) and in transit (TLS); centralized key management
              (KMS/HSM), rotation policies, dual control.

2.6.38.    Data-level Controls
            o  Column/field-level masking; row-level policies; hashing/tokenization for high-
                    risk PII where feasible.
            o  Stored  procedures  over  ad-hoc  queries;  input  validation; SQL  injection
                   protections (WAF + parameterized queries).
2.6.39.    Auditing & DAM
            o  Database Activity Monitoring (DAM) for privileged operations, schema changes,
                 bulk exports; alerts to SIEM; time-synced logging.
2.6.40.    Vulnerability & Patch
            o  Regular DB  vulnerability assessments;  critical  fixes within SLA; version
                 currency (N-2 or better unless MoCA/AAI defers).
2.6.41.   Backup & Recovery
            o  Encrypted backups; immutable copies; tested restores; retention aligned with
                   policy; secure disposal and legal holds supported.

2.6.43.    Centralized Telemetry
            o  Application, API, infrastructure, network, endpoint, database, and analytics

2.6.44.    Auditability
            o  Record at minimum: login/logout attempts, user transactions, privileged actions,

           actions recorded.

2.6.56.   Broad Security Requirements

         1.  Compliance: Conform to GoI guidance (e-Governance Security Guidelines, National
         Cyber Security Policy, GIGW 3.0, CERT-In Directions, DPDP Act 2023, etc.). Provide
           configurable RBAC/ABAC at application/module/transaction/data levels.
         2. PKI & Digital Signatures: Use for document/transaction integrity, non-repudiation, and
          sender identity as required.
         3.  Strong Authentication: Unique user IDs; SSO/MFA for privileged/sensitive functions;
           support digital signatures/biometrics/devices where applicable.
         4.  Passwords:  Enforce  length/complexity/passphrase policy; block  breached/common
          passwords; expiry/reuse policies as per MoCA/AAI standards.
         5.  Authorization & SoD: Fine-grained functional/data  rights; multi-level approval;
          workflows; SoD.
         6.  Data  Access  Controls:  Restrict  sensitive  elements  by  user/group/role;  field-
            level masking/format-preserving encryption where feasible.
         7.  Auditability: Tamper-evident logs and evidence trails; evolving requirements supported.
         8.  Audit  Logging: Log  login/logout,  user  transactions,  privileged/admin  changes,
           overrides, direct data changes, API access, and security events; time sync enforced.
         9.  Audit Queries & Retention: Filter/search by type/time/user/IP/host/API/key/correlation
           IDs; ≥
         180 days online retention; longer immutable archival per policy.
        10. Information Asset Classification: Classify and apply proportionate controls; all project
           data is owned by MoCA/AAI/AirSewa and shall not be disclosed to unauthorized parties.
        11. Malware/EDR:   On-access   and   periodic   scans;   signature/engine   updates;
           application allow-listing where feasible.
        12. Network & Segmentation: Robust segmentation and Zero Trust Across networks,
            servers, databases, endpoints, APIs, cloud.
        13. BCP & DR (On Cloud): Approved plans before  go-live; immutable backups;
           periodic DR/restore drills to validate RTO/RPO; DR drill with 50% load.

2.6.57.   IT Governance and Monitoring

 2.7. Overview of Project Governance

      The Service Provider (SP)  shall be  responsible  for  the end-to-end implementation,
       deployment, and stabilization of all modules, components, and sub-systems covered under
       the Scope of Work. A structured governance mechanism shall be established to ensure timely
       execution, transparent communication,  risk mitigation, and adherence  to quality and
       performance standards throughout the project  lifecycle. PMU/Authorized MoCA/AAI
        officials shall review and approve SRS, UI/UX, workflows, integrations, UAT sign-off and
      Go-Live readiness
2.7.1.     Project Monitoring Unit (PMU)

      The Agency shall establish a Project Monitoring Unit (PMU) for continuous review and
       monitoring of the project during the implementation phase.
      The PMU shall evaluate progress, verify milestone achievements, and assess compliance
       with contractual obligations and SLAs.
      The PMU shall coordinate with the Service Provider on timelines, deliverables, acceptance
        testing, and risk/escalation handling.
2.7.2.     Agency’s Single Point of Contact (SPOC)

      The Agency  shall appoint a dedicated Single Point of Contact (SPOC)  for  all IT
        administration-related activities. All project-related escalations from the SP shall be routed
       through this SPOC. The SPOC will be responsible for:
         o  Reviewing SLA adherence
         o  Overseeing issue resolution
         o  Coordinating with the PMU and other stakeholders
         o   Facilitating approvals, clarifications, and dependencies
      The  Service  Provider  must  prepare  and  submit  a  comprehensive  Customization,
       Implementation, and Rollout Plan, outlining:
         o  Functional customization approach
         o  Deployment methodology
         o   Integration timelines
         o  Resource allocation
         o  Training and UAT plans
         o  Rollout schedule Across all modules

   The CSP shall ensure that the AirSewa solution is designed and operated in line with the highest

  MoCA/AAI reserves the right to continuously monitor and inspect all inbound and outbound
    traffic Across hardware, software, applications, and network layers to identify, prevent, and
    mitigate any potential leakage of sensitive or configuration data.
    In case of identification of vulnerabilities or performance gaps in hardware/software provided
   by the CSP, MoCA/AAI may direct the CSP to replace the affected components with equivalent
    or superior alternatives at no additional cost to MoCA/AAI, at any point during the contract
    period.
   The envisaged solution shall mandatorily include:

        a) Data Masking and Sub-setting Capabilities – The ability to obfuscate and subset
            sensitive  information  while  enabling  secure  usage  Across  testing,  acceptance,
          development, and training environments.
       b) Automated Discovery of Sensitive Data – Identification of sensitive fields (columns,
            attributes) and maintenance of parent-child data relationships to ensure compliance with
           data protection requirements.
        c)  Secure Data Sub-setting – Selective deletion or exclusion of non-relevant or redundant
           records from databases to minimize exposure of sensitive information in non-production
          environments.
       d) Proactive Security Controls – Implementation of monitoring, auditing, logging, and
           incident response mechanisms to ensure end-to-end protection of CSP’s information
            assets.
   Any version upgrade/ changes in the solution shall be implemented in development  / test
   environment. The solution shall be migrated to the production environment only after adequate
    testing, submission of test completion certificate from MSP and approval by the designated
  MoCA/AAI officials. A quality assurance test of the changes to be implemented will be
   performed in a test environment prior to implementation in the production environment.
        e)  Others

   The envisaged  solution should  also ensure maintenance  of  data  integrity,  consistency,
   management of Data Lifecycle, compliance to industry standards. Audit trail and logging should
   be enabled in the envisaged solution.

            i.   The bidder shall study the functional requirements specifications and process flow as

        iv.   Bidder shall interact with the target end users as well as MoCA/AAI project team to
           gather requirements. It is expected that Bidder gathers requirements through structured
           questionnaires, focused interviews with user groups.
       v.  On gathering the requirements, Bidder shall analyse these requirements to ensure the
          requirements are complete, accurate, consistent and unambiguous.
        vi.    After the analysis, Bidder shall prepare software requirement specification (SRS)
          document. SRS shall contain the objectives and scope of the system, the various levels
           of requirements, the process model, data model, data dictionary, etc. Unresolved issues
            shall also be included in this document. User Role wise mapping to the various business
           functions with details regarding their access rights (insert / update / delete / view etc.)
            shall also be included in this document. Acceptance Criteria shall also be included
            explicitly promoting clear understanding with the end user about what the end user
           considers acceptable.
        vii.   The Bidder shall, as part of development of SRS (Software Requirement Specification),
          develop a system prototype to capture and demonstrate the end user requirements in the
         form of screens and outputs. The prototype shall be enhanced continuously during the
         development of SRS and SDD specifications.
       viii.   The SRS document shall be reviewed and approved by MoCA/AAI.

        ix.   Bidder shall develop a logical view of the solution to meet the user requirements. This
            logical view shall consist  - the functional architecture of the application and the
          changes/new  database  design.  Bidder  shall  also  define  standards  for  coding,
          documentation, user interfaces, etc., if the same is not already defined.
       x.   Bidder shall document the high-level design as System Design Document (SDD)
           consisting of project standards, the functional design and the database design. The SDD
         document shall be reviewed and approved by MoCA/AAI.

2.10.5.    Software Design specifications
                           i.    In this phase, Bidder shall develop a logical view of the solution to meet the user
                  requirements. This logical view shall consist - the functional architecture of the
                  application and the changes/new database design. Bidder shall also define
                  standards for coding, documentation, user interfaces, etc., if the same is not
                  already defined.

                            ii.   Bidder shall document the high-level design as System Design Document (SDD)
                  consisting of project standards, the functional design and the database design. The
           SDD document shall be reviewed and approved by MoCA/AAI.

2.10.6.    Preparation for Core Database design
                           i.   Bidder shall design the Logical Data Model of the Core Database based on the
                 understanding of the information needs of MoCA/AAI and existing information
                  items. The Logical Data Model shall include the definition of data structure or
               schema diagram and data dictionaries and metadata developed explaining specific
                     entities, relationships and dimensions identified as a part of the Logical Data
                Model.

2.10.7.    Build Stage – Coding and Unit Testing
                           i.   Bidder shall carry out detailed design (Low Level Design), Coding and Unit
                 Testing phases.

                            ii.   Bidder shall break down the high-level design into modules and programs. For
                 every program, a unit test plan shall be created. Bidder shall also identify common
                   routines, programs, utilities and tools for productivity improvement.

                             iii.   In this phase, high level design document is the main input whereas the program
                   specifications and unit test plans are the key outputs.

                    iv.  Bidder shall identify data migration programs that need to be developed and
                    tested. Since without them, programs for the current application cannot be tested
                  properly, it is important that Bidder develop and test them early.

2.10.8.    Documentation

           a)     Bidder shall develop manuals such as User Manuals, Training Manuals, FAQ &
               Trouble Shooting Guide, etc. as agreed upon with MoCA/AAI for different kinds
                of users such as System Administrator, Super user, Normal user.

           b)     Bidder  shall  ensure  that  the  documentation  is  in  line  with  the  defined
               documentation standards.

           c)     Bidder shall use tools for creation of Help documents, User manuals etc. that
              minimize the documentation and change efforts.

           d)     Bidder should share cloud utilization report monthly during the development and
         O&M stage. A separate cloud account should be created for the AirSewa project
               with access to the cloud account to MoCA/AAI for visibility.

           e)     System Integrator (SI) shall provide a monthly Cloud Utilization Report, including
                 detailed consumption metrics specific to the AirSewa project. The bidder must
                ensure that the AirSewa workload is maintained under a separate cloud account or
                 subscription to enable clear segregation of usage and cost. Access to the AirSewa
               cloud account shall be provided to the Ministry of Civil Aviation (MoCA/AAI) for
                    visibility, monitoring, and audit purposes.

               f)     The System Integrator (SI) shall provide logs, alerts, and corresponding reports
              from the deployed SIEM solution to confirm that no cybersecurity breaches,
                  intrusions, or security incidents have occurred within the environment. These logs
              must be complete, unaltered, time-stamped, and submitted as per the agreed
                 reporting frequency or upon request by the customer’s security team.

           g)    Any cybersecurity breaches, intrusions, application defects or security incidents
               should be notified as per SLAs and as part of the documentation.

           h)    The System Integrator shall provide PACR and Cloud Downtime logs generated
              from the available monitoring and management tools integrated with the cloud
               environment. These logs must clearly indicate any service outages, performance
                degradation events, maintenance windows, and uptime statistics for the specified
                 reporting period.

 2.11.    Ongoing Administration and Maintenance requirements Operational Support

   This  section  describes  the  operational  requirements  of  the  project  including  project
   management requirements, acceptance testing & certification and solution maintenance &
    support. The Bidder shall provide complete support for the entire IT infrastructure of the system
    including all the items supplied as part of the contract, for the contract period. The terms of
   warranty shall include that the solution supplied under this Contract shall have no defect arising
   from design or workmanship or from any act of omission or commission of the bidder or that
   may develop under normal use of the supplied solution Operational support shall ensure that
    the system is functioning as intended and meeting the service levels. Operational support will
    include:

          Bidder shall submit progress reports monthly. The progress report shall include the
           following:

                         i.   Tasks completed / Results achieved during the period (fortnight)

                         ii.   Tasks / Results to be completed in the subsequent period.

                         iii.   Cumulative deviations to date from schedule of progress on milestones

                 to planned schedule provided.

               v.   Other issues and outstanding problems, and actions to be taken from each

2.11.2.    Maintenance of user documents

          Bidder shall maintain the end user documents up to date as mentioned in the section

                         i.   Software solution maintenance

             The objective of application maintenance is to provide application maintenance
              and support services, including request-based services (problem requests/defect
                   fixes), enhancements, configuration management and post release support. As
                  part of these services, Bidder shall provide support for bug fixes, enhancements,
                 operational support, and assistance to MoCA/AAI. The services include:

                     a.  Bug-fixes and end-user problem resolution

                           The end user support shall include all activities related to resolving the
                          bugs/ defects reported by application users. Every bug / defect shall be
                          logged. Every bug / defect shall be categorized on the severity levels.

                           Bidder shall identify the solution and get the necessary approvals from
                  MoCA/AAI and release the patch for UAT after fixing the defects.

                           Bidder shall document defects / bugs encountered as well as document
                           the resolution of the same.

                           Ensure re-installations, in the event of system crash/failures.


                     b. New development and enhancements

                           The application may require modifications or enhancements in the
                             functionality. The enhancements or new development may also be
                           required to fix some complex problem requests or defect fixes.

                           Bidder shall ensure that correct versions of the application / program
                            units are being considered to carry out application enhancements/ new
                       development   through   configuration   management   plan   for
                           configuration management and version control using the version
                           control software.

                           Bidder shall support MoCA/AAI in carrying out the UAT for the
                          modifications / enhancements.


 2.12.     Operations and Maintenance

   The bidder will operate and maintain all the components of the AirSewa portal for a period of
    three (3) years from the date of Go-Live which may be extended by two (2) years on mutually
    agreeable terms. The Operation and Maintenance part is divided into the following parts:

2.12.1.    Application Support and Maintenance

   Application support includes, but not limited to, production monitoring, troubleshooting and
    addressing functionality/availability and performance issues and implementing system change
    requests etc. The bidder shall maintain the application software in good working conditions;
   and perform changes and upgrades to the applications as requested by the authority. All tickets
    related to any issue/complaint/observation about the system shall be maintained in an ITIL
   compliant comprehensive ticketing solution. Key activities to be performed by the bidder
   during the application support phase are as follows:

             i.   Compliance to SLA (in vol 3 of the document)

         The bidder shall ensure compliance with SLAs as indicated in this section of this RFP
         and any upgrades/major changes to the software shall be planned by the bidder while
          ensuring that the SLA requirements are met at no additional cost to the authority/
        MoCA/AAI.

            ii.   Annual Technology Support

         The bidder shall be responsible for arranging annual technology support to the AirSewa
           portal for the OEM products provided by respective OEMs during the entire O&M
           phase. It is mandatory for the bidder to take enterprise level annual support over the entire
           contract duration at minimum for the software(s) mentioned below:

                  a.  Operating System

                  b.  Virtualization layers

                   c.  Database

                  d.  Analytics tool

                  e.  All third-party products/ engines deployed in the AIRSEWA

             iii.   Application Software Maintenance



                                     Page 97 of 215

<!-- Page 98 -->

    The  bidder  shall  provide  continuous  and  indefinite  support  through  on-site
     team/telephone/E-mail/Video Conferencing/installation visits as required. The bidder
     shall address all the errors/bugs/gaps in the functionalities of the solution (vis-a-vis the
    FRS, BRD and SRS signed off) at no additional cost during the O&M phase.

     All patches and upgrades from OEMs shall be implemented by the bidder. Technical
    upgrades of installation to the new version, as and when required, shall be done by the
     bidder. Any version upgrades of the software/tool/application will be done by the bidder
      after seeking prior approval from the MoCA/AAI and submitting the impact assessment
     of an upgrade as such.

   Any changes/upgrades to the software performed during the support phase shall be
     subject to comprehensive and integrated testing by the bidder to ensure that the changes
    implemented in the system meet the specified requirements and do not impact any other
     existing functions of the system. A detailed process in this regard will be finalised by
     the bidder in consultation with the MoCA/AAI.

   An Issue log shall be maintained by the bidder for the errors and bugs identified in the
     solution as well as any changes implemented in the solution and shall be periodically
     submitted to the MoCA/AAI.

    The  bidder  will  inform  the MoCA/AAI  (at  least  monthly)  about  any new
     updates/upgrades available for all software components of the solution along with a
     detailed action report. In case of critical security patches/alerts, the bidder shall inform
     the MoCA/AAI immediately along with any relevant

    recommendations. The report shall also contain the bidder’s recommendations on
     update/upgrade,  benefits,  impact  analysis  etc.  The  bidder  needs  to  execute
     updates/upgrades though a formal change management process and subsequently
     update all documentations and Knowledge databases etc. The bidder will carry out all
     required updates/upgrades by following defined processes at no additional cost.

iv.   Database Administration, Maintenance and Management Services

       a.  Management of database environment to maintain performance at optimum levels

       b.  End-to-end management of database on an ongoing basis to ensure smooth
         functioning of the same.

       c.  Tasks including, but not limited to, managing changes to database schema, disk
         space, storage, user roles etc.

       d.  Conduct code and configuration reviews to provide inputs to MoCA/AAI to
        improve the performance or resolve bottlenecks if any.

       e.  Performance monitoring and tuning of the databases on a regular basis including,
         preventive maintenance of the database as required.

          f.  Report backup status on a regular basis and ensure prompt problem resolution in
         case of failures in the backup processes.

                                Page 98 of 215

<!-- Page 99 -->

       g.  Manage database upgrade or patch upgrade as and when required with minimal
         downtime.

       h.  Regular backups for all databases in accordance with the backup and archive
          policies and recovery whenever required with appropriate permissions. Testing of
        backup media should be undertaken from time to time to ensure availability of data.
         Bidder should coordinate with MoCA/AAI for back-up activities

 v.   Problem identification and Resolution:

       a.  Errors and bugs that persist for a long time, impact a wider range of users and are
           difficult to resolve in turn lead to application hindrances. The bidder shall resolve all
          the application problems through implementation of the identified solution (e.g.
         system malfunctions, performance problems and data corruption etc.)

       b.  Monthly reports on problems identified and resolved would be submitted to the
       MoCA/AAI team along with recommended solutions.

 vi.   Change and Version Control

       All planned or emergency changes to any component of the system shall be carried
       out through the approved Change Management process. The bidder needs to follow
        all such processes (always based on industry ITSM framework). For any change,
       bidder shall ensure:

         a.  Detailed impact analysis is conducted
         b.  All Change plans are backed by Roll back plans

          c.  Appropriate communication on change required has taken place

         d.  Requisite approvals have been received

         e.  Schedules have been adjusted to minimize impact on the Production environment

             f.  All associated documentation is updated post stabilization of the implemented
          change

         g.  Version control is maintained for all software changes

     The bidder shall define the Software Change Management and Version Control
       Process. For any changes  to  the  solution,  the  bidder must  prepare  detailed
      documentation including proposed changes and impact to the system in terms of
       functional outcomes/additional features added to the system etc. The bidder shall
      ensure that software and hardware version control is carried out for the entire contract
       duration.

vii.   Maintain configuration information

     The bidder shall maintain version control and configuration information for application
      software and any relevant system documentation.

  The bidder shall design, update and maintain all the System documentations of the AirSewa
   portal. These documents shall be up to date and shall be shared MoCA/AAI on the regular
   basis. The bidder should follow latest industry standards to design and maintain the
   documentations.
  The bidder shall maintain at least the following minimum documentation with respect to
   the AirSewa portal:

          b. Low level design for whole system/module design level

          c.  System Requirements Specifications (SRS)

          d. Any other explanatory notes about system

          e.  Traceability matrix

              f.  Compilation environment

     The bidder shall also ensure that any software system documentation  is updated
      regarding the following:
          a.  Source code is documented

          b.  Functional specifications are documented

          c.  Application documentation is  updated  to  reflect on-going maintenance and

          d.  User  manuals  and   training  manuals  are  updated   to   reflect  on-
           going changes/enhancements

          e.  Standard practices of version control and management are adopted and followed

 ix.    All project documents need to follow a proper version control mechanism. The bidder

2.12.2.   Web based SLA Monitoring Tool

   Bidder should deploy Web based SLA Monitoring Tool for monitoring all the SLAs as
    specified in Volume 3 of the RFP. The indicative list of features to be provided under the SLA
   Monitoring is provided below. Bidder must provide monitoring mechanism for all the SLAs
   mentioned in Volume 3 of the RFP.

         a.  The SLA Monitoring tool shall be tested, audited and certified for  its accuracy,
             reliability and completeness before it is implemented by the bidder.
         b.  The selected tool must be from latest Gartner’s magic quadrant leader’s list/equivalent
           position
           in Forrester wave/ leader in IDC marketspace (As a supporting document

         c.   the bidder must provide for relevant report of latest Gartner’s magic quadrant leader’s
                list/
           equivalent position in Forrester wave/report of IDC).


                                    Page 101 of 215

    d.  The MoCA/AAI will periodically conduct quality reviews (preferably quarterly) to
       ensure that the tool is monitoring SLAs properly.
    e.  The bidder will work with MoCA/AAI officials to design these reports as per specific
       requirements. The solution provides both real time dashboards and historical view
       reporting of the SLA's at to the relevant stakeholders of MoCA/AAI.
      f.  The SLA monitoring tool should be able to monitor all the service levels defined in the
       service level agreement.
    g.  The SLA Monitoring tool should be web based

    h.  The tool should be also accessed by smart devices over internet/local airport LAN

       i.  The SLA monitoring tool will be tested in the Acceptance Phase for acceptance

       j.  The proposed tool should use smart visualization and graphics


Some of the minimum indicative features of the SLA Monitoring tool includes

    a.  Integrated performance view of all the SLAs along with various threshold violations
        for each service level.

    b.  Indicate the baseline, breach, lower and high-performance scores against each of the
       service level metric in a graphical form.

     c.  Dashboard must show bidder action taken on each of the SLA breach/ timelines for
       resolution etc.

    d.  The proposed tool should enable users to generate Pre-defined, Standard and Ad-hoc
        reports

    e.  Allow users to generate report with pre-defined fields to get a real-time view of the
      system from the portal.

      f.  Pre-defined Reports will be generated at pre-defined frequencies and will be available
        for to the users

    g.  Standard reports would include reports where report generation criteria are fixed and
       options for the same are available to the User. The User can select a combination of
       options and submits a request for generation of a report. The system generated the report
      and the same would be made available on the User dashboard.

    h. Ad hoc Reports: These reports can be generated by the User by raising ad hoc queries.
      These would provide the ability to generate ad hoc reports as and when required by
       selecting any field, table or column (as per Database design) by drag and drop feature
      from the table.

2.12.3.    Application Monitoring Services

   The bidder should ensure exceptional end-user experience and consistently high service levels
    that meet business objectives by monitoring all end-user transactions 24x7 with low overhead.
   The  application performance monitoring  solution  should  accurately measure  end-user
    transaction performance to prove IT is delivering against SLAs, business objectives and vendor
   commitments. APM should be deployed with the objective of improving IT productivity and
    control costs by quickly and accurately diagnosing problems occurring deep within the
    application and infrastructure, pinpoint failures and speed problem resolution.

   The services to be provided by the bidder for Application Monitoring which includes following
    but not limited to:

         a. Web/ API services

         b.  Application server

         c.  Database server

         d.  Middleware

         e.  Value Added Services

             f.  Other components as proposed by bidder

   Application monitoring should map all transactions to the dependent infrastructure in real time
    for a single view of application health, business process flow and the entire transaction path to
   quickly triage issues

2.12.4.    Storage Administration & Management Services (by Cloud Service Provider (CSP))

         a.  Management of storage environment to maintain performance at optimum levels.

         b.  Management of the storage solution including, but not limited to, storage management
           policy, configuration and management of disk array, SAN, tape library, etc.
         c.  Storage management, including but not limited to management of space, volume,
         Redundant Array of Inexpensive Disks configuration, LUN, zone, security, business
           continuity volumes, performance, etc
2.12.5.   Workflow Management:

                  a.  Workflow management module shall enable the workflows for various business
                  processes. This shall include process tasks and routing. Workflow shall allow
                  configuring various users such as Airport operators, Airlines, etc. into the
                  business processes.

2.12.6.   Backup and Archival Services

      The services to be provided by bidder shall include:

         a.  Backup of storage as per the defined policies.

         b.  Monitoring and enhancing the performance of scheduled backups, schedule regular
            testing of backups and ensuring adherence to related retention policies as defined by
         AAI, DGCA, MoCA/AAI, and other relevant authorities.
         c.  Prompt execution of on-demand backups of volumes and files whenever required or in
          case of upgrades and configuration changes to the system.
         d.  Real-time monitoring, log maintenance and reporting of backup status on a regular basis.

         e.  Media management tasks, including, but not limited to, tagging, cross-referencing,
            storing, logging, testing, and vaulting in fireproof cabinets (onsite and offsite).
             f.  365x24x7 support for file and volume restoration requests at the cloud (VPC)

2.12.7.    Technical Support Chatbot

   The technical support track includes running centralised helpdesk setup and operations for
  AIRSEWA project for a period of 36 months from the date of Go Live.

         1.  The help desk will primarily handle technical queries of internal stakeholders which
           includes Airport Operators, CISF Staff, Airlines, OTAs, etc. relating to AIRSEWA.

         2.  The bidder shall follow the implementation plan for the helpdesk launch as specified in
           the project timelines.

         3.  Helpdesk is required to ensure that the above-mentioned stakeholders can log calls and
          complaints for any technical issues they face while using the AIRSEWA. The following
             is included in the scope of work of bidder:

         4.  Landline/mobile number will be taken by bidder in MoCA/AAI name. This number will
          be published, and internal stakeholders can call this number for their issues.

         5.  Help Desk to have Interactive Voice Response (IVR) system for first level of call
           segregation.

         6.  In addition to the telephone call, the bidder shall also provide other channels for call
          logging like email and web interface.

         7.  Language Capabilities: Hindi and English

         8.  The bidder shall deploy helpdesk application accessible to  all users through the
       AIRSEWA for logging issues.

         9.  The service window for Help Desk is 365X24X7 (Monday to Sunday).

                                    Page 104 of 215

        10. The helpdesk is also expected to handle queries after their go live. The help desk will
           serve as single point of contact for reporting/resolution of all tickets (queries, errors,
            incidents, issues either application or infrastructure or operations related).

        11. Considering the nature of the services, the bidder should decide for imparting proper
            training in soft skills; call handling, exposure to related application to prepare the CSEs
            to answer different types of queries, and on other aspects of Help Desk.

        12. The bidder should ensure that all the staffs are put on actual duty only after providing
         them proper training

        13. Before deployment of staff, he/she need to undergo training for at least a week time
          covering above areas. Certificate of such training need to be submitted on quarterly
           basis to MoCA/AAI.

      The helpdesk would have following major activities and tasks:

         1.  Issues logged by users through Helpdesk should be accessed and serviced by the
          helpdesk personnel.

         2.  Track and route requests for service and to assist end users in answering questions
         and resolving problems. Assign severity level to each ticket as per the SOPs.

         3.  Acknowledgement should be sent to user along with service ticket number through an
          email immediately on call logging.

         4.  Routing the query received to the concerned team of the bidder for resolution of tickets


         5.  Notifying users the problem status and resolution through the tickets over email.

           arising out of or in connection with the Agreement or the interpretation thereof.

          out of the Agreement or breach, termination, shall firstly be attempted to be settled by
           conciliator appointed/ nominated by AAI on receipt of such requests from either party.

           final and binding on the parties. The settlement agreement shall have the same status and
           effect of an Arbitration Award under the Arbitration and Conciliation Act 1996. If the
           conciliation failed, the party may refer the matter to Arbitration to resolve the disputes.

2.24 Service Level Agreement (SLA)

   2.24.1.  SLA for AirSewa

   For purposes of the SLA, the definitions and terms as specified in the document along with the
   following terms shall have the meanings set forth below:

          I.   “Downtime” – Time period for which the specified services/ components/ outcomes
          are not available in the concerned period which would exclude downtime owing to
         Force Majeure, scheduled maintenance time (approved by AAI).

         II.    “Incident” – Any event/ abnormalities in the service being rendered, that may lead to
          disruption in normal operations and services to the end user.

        III.    “Ticket Resolution time” - means time taken by the Helpdesk to resolve (diagnosing,
          troubleshooting and fixing) a service request for any incident.

     IV.    “Ticket Closure time”- This includes the ticket resolution time and approval time taken
        by department post resolution of ticket. If it is identified that issue is not resolved then
          “Ticket resolution Time” will be addition of Ticket closure time and actual ticket
           resolution time for SLA computation.

     V.   “Scheduled Maintenance Time” – Time period for which the specified services/
          Application is not available due to scheduled maintenance activity pre-approved by
        AAI.

     VI.    “Service Request” - Request from a user for reporting incident or to seek support,
           delivery, information, advice or documentation.

     VII.    “Total Time” – Total number of hours in the quarter (or the concerned period) being
          considered for evaluation of SLA performance.
                                      Page 121 of 215

<!-- Page 122 -->

    VIII.   "Uptime" – Time period for which the specified services/ outcomes are available in the
          period being considered for evaluation of SLA. Formulae for calculation of Uptime:

            (%) = {1−                                                    } x 100%  Theu𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝𝑝prime objective of(𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷𝐷/(service levels𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇𝑇is to 𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡−𝑠𝑠𝑠𝑠ℎ𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒𝑒ensure quality of services𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚𝑚from the𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡𝑡)Bidder, in an
   efficient manner as defined in RFP. To meet this objective, the bidder will provide the Service
   Levels in accordance with the performance levels as set out in detail in this Section i.e., Service
   Level Agreement.

  The service level targets define the levels of service to be provided by the bidder to AAI for the
   duration of this contract or until the stated SLA targets are amended.

   2.24.2.  General Principles of SLA:

        I.   This Agreement shall govern the provision of the Contracted professional services of
          the bidder to the AAI.

       II.  The bidder shall comply to service level requirements for availability and quality of
           services, throughout the period of the Contract or extensions, if any.

      III.  24x7 shall mean hours between 12:00 AM -11.59 PM midnight on all days of the week.

    IV.  The bidder needs to perform periodic maintenance/upgradation on hosting environment
           for the purposes of system upgrades, maintenance, and backup procedures. The
         maintenance can be done only on Scheduled Maintenance Time with at least 3 days
           prior approval from AAI. However, no downtime will be allowed for the critical
         modules. For those  services, the  bidder needs  to  devise a mechanism  for  the
          uninterrupted services even in case of any maintenance and updates through DR sites.

    V.   The purpose of imposing penalty on account of SLA is to ensure the performance of
          the services as per defined parameters. In view of this, if the performance of the services
             is not improved, it shall constitute enough grounds for the annulment of the Contract.

2.25 Liquidated Damages

  Time is the essence of the project and the delivery dates are binding on the Bidder. In the event
   of delay or any  gross  negligence  in implementation  of  the  project  for  causes  solely
   attributable to the B i d d e r , in achieving the milestones, the AAI shall be entitled to recover
   from the bidder as liquidated damages. Delays that are not attributed to bidder shall not be
   considered for the penalty.

  The liquidated damages subject to a maximum of 10% of the Contract Value.

    If the cumulative liquidated damages go beyond 10% of the Contract value, AAI reserves the
   right to take appropriate action including termination.

   This right to claim any liquidated damages shall be without prejudice to other rights and
   remedies available to AAI under the Contract and law.


                                      Page 122 of 215

<!-- Page 123 -->

The applicable Liquidated damages may be recovered by deduction of the same value from first
or any invoice of CAPEX or OPEX as defined in payment terms. Any such recovery or
liquidated damages shall not in any way relieve the bidder from any of its obligations to
complete the Work or from any other obligations and liabilities under the Contract.

The SLA parameters shall be monitored on a quarterly basis as per the individual SLA
parameter requirements while reporting shall be monthly or instance/incident/event bases.
However, if the performance of the system/services is degraded significantly at any given point
in time during the contract and if the immediate measures are not implemented and issues are
not rectified to the complete satisfaction of AAI or an agency designated by them, then AAI
will have the right to take appropriate disciplinary actions including termination of the contract.

2.27 Incident Management SLAs

   Severity of Outages / Application defects is defined as below –

      •  Severity 1: Critical

     Showstoppers involving major functional failure in the application. There are no usable
     workarounds available to resolve the problem. Affects majority of the users and airports
     (more than 25%).

      •  Severity 2: High

     Users face severe functional defects in the application irrespective of the cause. Workarounds
       is available. Affects majority of the users (more than 25%).

      •  Severity 3: Moderate

     Moderate functional defects in the application irrespective of the cause. Has a convenient and
      readily available workaround. Affects a few users (less than 25%)

      •  Severity 4: Low

     Requiring cosmetic functional changes. Does not require any workaround. It may include
      user query / suggestions but has no business impact.

   Penalty applicable on bidder as per the breach of SLA for Incident Management

                   will be compensated for additional percentage or new tax.

                  GOVERNANCE SCHEDULE
1. PURPOSE

   The purpose of this Schedule is to:

     i.    establish and maintain the formal and informal processes for managing the relationship
      between AAI and the bidder (including the outputs from other Schedules to this
      Agreement;

     ii.    define the principles that both Parties wish to follow to ensure the delivery of the Services;

    iii.    ensure the continued alignment of the interests of the Parties;

  iv.   ensure that the relationship is maintained at the correct level within each Party;

   v.    create the flexibility to revise and maintain the relationship and this Agreement during the
      Term;

  vi.     set out the procedure for escalating disagreements; and

  vii.    enable contract administration and performance management.


2. GOVERNANCE STRUCTURE


       i.    Project Managers: The relationship under this Agreement will be managed by the
        Project Managers appointed by each Party, who will provide the interface between the
        executive management of the respective Parties.

      ii.    Project Implementation Unit (PIU): Within 7 days following the Effective Date, AAI
        or its nominated agencies and the bidder shall each appoint a Project Manager. In the
        event that either Party wishes to substitute its Project Manager it will do so in manner
        in which the original appointment  is made and notify the other Party of such
        substitution as soon as reasonably practicable but at the latest within 7 days of the
         substitution.

     iii.   The Project Managers shall have responsibility for maintaining the interface and
       communication between the Parties.





                                   Page 144 of 215

<!-- Page 145 -->

 iv.   The PIU will meet formally on a fortnightly / monthly / quarterly, as required, basis at
      a time and location to be agreed between them. These meetings will cover, as a
     minimum, the following agenda items: (i) consideration of Quarterly Performance
      Reports; (ii) consideration of matters arising out of the Change Control Schedule; (iii)
       issues escalated in accordance with the escalation procedure as  set out in the
      Governance Schedule; (iv) matters to be brought before the PIU in accordance with the
    MSA and the Schedules; (v) any matter brought before the PIU by the bidder under this
       Article; and (vi) any other issue which either Party wishes to add to the agenda.

  v.    In the event that there is any material factor which affects the delivery of the Services
       or the terms of payment as stated in the Terms of Payment Schedule, the Parties agree
       to discuss in the PIU any appropriate amendment to the Agreement or any Service
      Level Agreements or Statement of Works including any variation to the terms of
      payment as stated in the Terms of Payment Schedule. Any variation so agreed shall be
      implemented through the change control procedure as set out in the Change Control
      Schedule.

 vi.   The other Party ("Respondent") shall have the right to respond to the Dispute Notice
      within 7 days after receipt of the Dispute Notice. In the event that the parties are unable
       to resolve the Disputed Matter within a further period of 7 days, it shall refer the
      Disputed Matter to next level of the dispute resolution for action as per the process
      mentioned.

 vii.    All negotiations, statements and / or documentation pursuant to these Articles shall be
      without prejudice and confidential (unless mutually agreed otherwise).

viii.     If the Disputed Matter is having a material effect on the operation of the Services (or
      any of them or part of them) the Parties will use all their respective reasonable
      endeavours to reduce the elapsed time in reaching a resolution of the Disputed Matter.

                   AUDIT, ACCESS AND REPORTING


1. PURPOSE

This Schedule details the audit, access and reporting rights and obligations of AAI or its
nominated agency and the System Integrator.


2. AUDIT NOTICE AND TIMING

    2.1 As soon as reasonably practicable after the Effective Date, the Parties shall use their
       best endeavours  to agree  to a timetable  for  routine  audits during  the  Project
       Implementation Phase and the Operation and Management Phase. Such timetable
       during the Implementation Phase, AAI or its nominated agency and thereafter during
       the operation Phase, AAI or its nominated agency shall conduct routine audits in
       accordance with such agreed timetable and shall not be required to give the bidder any
        further notice of carrying out such audits. Exit Management plan shall be furnished in
       writing to AAI or its nominated agencies within 90 days from the Effective Date of this
      Agreement.

    2.2 AAI or its nominated agency may conduct non-timetabled audits at his/ her own
        discretion if it reasonably believes that such non-timetabled audits are necessary as a
        result of an act of fraud by the System Integrator, a security violation, or breach of
        confidentiality obligations by the System Integrator, provided that the requirement for
       such an audit is notified in writing to the bidder a reasonable period time prior to the
        audit (taking into account the circumstances giving rise to the reasonable belief) stating
        in a reasonable level of detail the reasons for the requirement and the alleged facts on
      which the requirement is based. If the bidder considers that the non-timetabled audit
      was not appropriate, the matter shall be referred to the escalation procedure as set out in
       the Governance Schedule.

    2.3 The frequency of audits shall be a (maximum) half yearly, provided always that AAI or
         its nominated agency shall endeavour to conduct such audits with the lowest levels of
       inconvenience and disturbance practicable being caused to the System Integrator. Any
       such audit shall be conducted by with adequate notice of 2 weeks to the System
        Integrator.

    2.4 AAI will ensure that any 3rd party agencies (except CAG) appointed to conduct the audit
        will not be the competitor of bidder and will be bound by confidentiality obligations.


3. ACCESS


                                   Page 146 of 215

<!-- Page 147 -->

   The bidder shall provide to AAI or its nominated agency reasonable access to employees,
   JV/consortium members, suppliers, agents and third party facilities as detailed in the RFP,
   documents, records and systems reasonably required for audit and shall provide all such
   persons with routine assistance in connection with the audits and inspections. The
    Chairperson, PIU / Steering Committee shall have the right to copy and retain copies of
   any relevant records. The bidder shall make every reasonable effort to co-operate with
   them.


4. AUDIT RIGHTS

    4.1 AAI or its nominated agency shall have the right to audit and inspect suppliers, agents
      and third party facilities (as detailed in the RFP), data centres, documents, records,
       procedures and systems relating to the provision of the services, but only to the extent
        that they relate to the provision of the services, as shall be reasonably necessary to
        verify:

     i.   The security, integrity and availability of all data processed, held or conveyed by the
       Partner on behalf of AAI and documentation related thereto;

     ii.   That the actual level of performance of the services is the same as specified in the SLA;

    iii.   That the bidder has complied with the relevant technical standards, and has adequate
        internal controls in place; and

  iv.   The compliance of the bidder with any other obligation under the MSA and SLA.

   v.    Security audit and implementation audit of the system shall be done once each year, the
       cost of which shall be borne by the System Integrator.

  vi.   For the avoidance of doubt the audit rights under this Schedule shall not include access
        to the System Integrator's profit margins or overheads, any confidential information
        relating to the System Integrator’ employees, or (iii) minutes of its internal Board or

  vii.   Board committee meetings including internal audit, or (iv) such other information of
       commercial-in-confidence nature which are not relevant to the Services associated with
      any obligation under the MSA.


5.  AUDIT RIGHTS OF JV/CONSORTIUM MEMBERS, SUPPLIERS AND AGENTS

    5.1 The bidder shall use reasonable endeavours to achieve the same audit and access
       provisions as defined in this Schedule with JV/consortium member who supply labour,
       services in respect of the services. The bidder shall inform AAI or its nominated agency
        prior to concluding any JV/consortium or supply agreement of any failure to achieve
       the same rights of audit or access

    5.2 REPORTING: The bidder will provide quarterly reports to the Chairperson, PIU  /
       Steering committee regarding any specific aspects of the Project and in context of the
        audit and access information as required by AAI or its nominated agency.


6. ACTION AND REVIEW


    6.1 Any change or amendment to the systems and procedures of the bidder, where
       applicable arising from the audit report shall be agreed within thirty (30) calendar days
      from the submission of the said report.

    6.2 Any  discrepancies  identified by any  audit  pursuant  to  this Schedule  shall be
       immediately notified to AAI or its nominated agency and the bidder Project Manager who
        shall determine what action should be taken in respect of such discrepancies in
       accordance with the terms of the MSA


7. TERMS OF PAYMENT

AAI shall bear the cost of any audits and inspections. The terms of payment are exclusive of
any costs of the bidder and the JV/consortium, for all reasonable assistance and information
provided under the MSA, the Project Implementation, Operation and Management SLA by the
bidder pursuant to the Schedule. All licenses, subscriptions, warranties and assets procured
under the project shall be in the name of AAI. Pending payments may be withheld in case of
unresolved license/subscription transfer issues during Exit Management

8. RECORDS AND INFORMATION

For the purposes of audit in accordance with this Schedule, the bidder shall maintain true and
accurate records in connection with the provision of the services and the bidder shall handover
all the relevant records and documents upon the termination or expiry of the MSA

                  EXIT MANAGEMENT SCHEDULE
1. PURPOSE

        1.1 This Schedule sets out the provisions, which will apply on expiry or termination of
           the contract, the Project Implementation, Operation and Management SLA.

        1.2 In the case of termination of the Project Implementation and/or Operation and
         Management, the Parties shall agree at that time whether, and if so during what
           period, the provisions of this Schedule shall apply.

        1.3 The Parties shall ensure that their respective associated entities carry out their
           respective obligations set out in this Exit Management Schedule.


2  TRANSFER OF ASSETS

        2.1 AAI shall be entitled to serve notice in writing on the bidder at any time during the
            exit management period as detailed hereinabove requiring the bidder and/or its
          JV/consortium member to provide AAI with a complete and up to date list of the
          Assets within 30 days of such notice. AAI shall then be entitled to serve notice in
           writing on the bidder at any time prior to the date that is 30 days prior to the end of
           the exit management period requiring the bidder to sell the Assets, if any, to be
           transferred to AAI or its nominated agencies at book value as determined as of the
           date of such notice in accordance with the provisions of relevant laws.

        2.2 In case of contract being terminated by AAI, AAI reserves the right to ask bidder to
          continue running the project operations for a period of 6 months after termination
           orders are issued.

        2.3 Upon service of a notice under this Article the following provisions shall apply:

                              i.    in the event, if the Assets to be transferred are mortgaged to any financial
                      institutions by the bidder, the bidder shall ensure that all such liens and
                        liabilities have been cleared beyond doubt, prior to such transfer. All
                 documents regarding the discharge of such lien and liabilities shall be
                   furnished to AAI.

                              ii.    All risk in and title to the Assets to be transferred / to be purchased by AAI
                   pursuant to this Article shall be transferred to AAI, on the last day of the
                     exit management period.

                              iii.   AAI shall pay to the bidder on the last day of the exit management period
                 such sum representing the Net Block (procurement price less depreciation
                   as per provisions Of Companies Act) of the Assets to be transferred as
                     stated in the Terms of Payment Schedule.

                CHANGE CONTROL SCHEDULE

1    Introducing a Change

1.1  AAI shall have the right to propose, and subsequently require, the Project Manager to
      order the Supplier from time to time during the performance of the Contract to make any
     change, modification, addition, or deletion to, in, or from the System (interchangeably
      called Change), provided that such Change falls within the general scope of the System,
     does not constitute unrelated work, and is technically practicable, taking into account
     both the state of advancement of the System and the technical compatibility of the
     Change envisaged with the nature of the System as originally specified in the Contract.

   A Change may involve, but is not restricted to, the substitution of updated Information
     Technologies and related Services in accordance with system/product Upgrades.

1.2  The Supplier may from time to time during its performance of the Contract propose to
    AAI (with a copy to the Project Manager) any Change that the Supplier considers
      necessary or desirable to improve the quality or efficiency of the System. AAI may at its
      discretion approve or reject any Change proposed by the Supplier.

1.3   Notwithstanding Clauses 1.1.1. and 1.1.2 of Schedule I of this document, no change made
      necessary because of any default of the Supplier in the performance of its obligations
     under the Contract shall be deemed to be a Change, and such change shall not result in
     any extra cost or Time for Achieving Operational Acceptance.

1.4  The procedure on how to proceed with and execute Changes is specified below.

1.5   Moreover, AAI and Supplier will agree, during development of the Project Plan, to a date
      prior to the scheduled date for Operational Acceptance,  after which the Technical
     Requirements for the System shall be frozen. Any major Change initiated after this time
      will be dealt with after Operational Acceptance.

2    Changes originating from AAI

2.1    If AAI proposes a Change pursuant to Clause mentioned, it shall send to the Supplier a
      request for Change Proposal,‖ requiring the Supplier to prepare and furnish to the Project
     Manager as soon as reasonably practicable a Change Proposal, which shall include the
      following:

           •   brief description of the Change;
           •  impact on the Time for Achieving Operational Acceptance;
           •   detailed estimated cost of the Change;
           •   effect on Functional Guarantees (if any);
           •   Effect on any other provisions of the Contract.


                                   Page 154 of 215

<!-- Page 155 -->

     2.2   Prior to preparing and submitting the Change Proposal, the Supplier shall submit to the
           Project Manager a Change Estimate Proposal, which shall be an estimate of the cost of
          preparing the Change Proposal, plus a first approximation of the suggested approach and
           cost for implementing the changes. Upon receipt of the Supplier‘s Change Estimate
          Proposal, AAI shall do one of the following:

     2.2.1   Accept the Supplier‘s estimate with instructions to the Supplier to proceed with the preparation

            of the Change Proposal;

     2.2.2   Advise the Supplier of any part of its Change Estimate Proposal that is unacceptable and
            request the Supplier to review its estimate;

     2.2.3   Advise the Supplier that AAI does not intend to proceed with the Change.

     2.2.4  Upon receipt of AAI‘s instruction to proceed under Clause mentioned, the Supplier shall,
           with proper expedition, proceed with the preparation of the Change Proposal, in accordance
           with Clause mentioned .The Supplier, at its discretion, may specify a validity period for
            the Change

Proposal, after which if AAI and Supplier has not reached agreement in accordance with Clauses
mentioned shall apply.

     2.2.5  The pricing of any Change shall, as far as practicable, be calculated in accordance with the
             rates and prices included in the Contract. If the nature of the Change is such that the Contract
             rates and prices are inequitable, the parties to the Contract shall agree on other specific rates
            to be used for valuing the Change.

     2.2.6  Upon receipt of the Change Proposal, AAI and the Supplier shall mutually agree upon all
            matters contained in the Change Proposal. Within fourteen (14) days after such agreement,
        AAI shall, if it intends to proceed with the Change, issue the Supplier a Change Order. If
        AAI is unable to reach a decision within fourteen (14) days, it shall notify the Supplier with
             details of when the Supplier can expect a decision. If AAI decides not to proceed with the
          Change for whatever reason, it shall, within the said period of fourteen (14) days, notify the
           Supplier accordingly. Under such circumstances, the Supplier shall not be entitled to
           reimbursement of any costs incurred by it in the preparation of the Change Proposal.

     2.2.7    If AAI and the Supplier cannot reach agreement on the price for the Change, an equitable
           adjustment to the Time for Achieving Operational Acceptance, or any other matters
             identified in the Change Proposal, the Change will not be implemented.

3    Changes originating from Bidder

3.1    If the bidder proposes a Change pursuant to Clause mentioned, the bidder shall submit to
      the project manager a written application for Change Proposal, giving reasons for the
     proposed Change and including the information specified in Clause mentioned. Upon
      receipt of the application for Change Proposal, the Parties shall follow the procedures
      outlined in Clauses mentioned. However, should AAI/ AirSewa and PACR project
     committee (comprising relevant stakeholders as per discretion of AAI) choose not to
     proceed or AirSewa and PACR project committee and the bidder cannot come to
      Contract on the change during any validity period that the bidder may specify in its
      application for Change Proposal, the bidder shall not be entitled to recover the costs of
      preparing the application for Change Proposal, unless subject to an Contract between
    AAI and the bidder to the contrary.

3.2  The bidder needs to make any changes in software suggested by AAI or the bidder in
     accordance with Clause mentioned. It is expected that any minor changes shall be carried
      out by bidder without any extra cost. In case of substantial change requests, extra cost
      estimated for the change shall be paid by AAI.

3.3  A Change control board is to be constituted comprising of technical team members from
    AAI

3.4     . The change management process as per ITIL 4 will apply. The details of the
     change management process will be defined in the inception phase.

4    Acceptance Criteria

4.1   Acceptance of Software
1. General
     i.   The system shall support secure, role-based access for at least the following roles:

       Complainants, Redressors, Redressor Administrators, Air Sewa Agents and AAI
        officers

     ii.   The system shall be usable on modern web browsers (Latest LTS version of
      Chrome, Edge, Firefox) and responsive on standard mobile devices Android and
      iOS)

    iii.    All acceptance criteria shall be testable with clear pass/fail outcomes.

2. Grievance Intake and Registration

     i.   The system shall allow users to submit grievances via web portal, mobile app,
       chatbot and other inbound channels as outlined in the scope of work

     ii.   The system shall capture at minimum: complainant identity (or anonymous
        flag),  contact  details,  grievance  category  and  subcategory,  description,
        date/time, location (if applicable), and supporting documents.

    iii.   The system shall auto-generate a unique grievance ID immediately upon
       successful submission and display/communicate it to the complainant.

  iv.   The system shall validate required fields, show user-friendly error messages, and
       prevent submission until mandatory data is provided.

   v.   The system shall send an acknowledgment (on-screen and via email and SMS)

  vi.   The system shall allow saving a draft grievance and resuming later.

3. Workflow, Routing, and SLAs
     i.   The system shall automatically route new grievances to the appropriate
       Redressor Officer or queue based on configured business rules.

     ii.   The system shall support configurable workflow states (e.g., New, Under
      Review,  Pending  Clarification,  Resolved,  Closed,  Escalated)  and
       associated transitions.

    iii.   The system shall support configurable SLA definitions per category or
        priority (e.g., acknowledgment within X hours, first response within Y
       days, resolution within Z days).




                               Page 158 of 215

<!-- Page 159 -->

  iv.   The system shall automatically flag and highlight SLA breaches and
       near-breaches in Redressor Operator and Admin dashboard and to the
     AAI dashboard.

   v.   The system shall support manual reassignment and escalation with audit
       logging of who changed what and when.

  vi.   The system shall support multi-level escalation paths (e.g., Officer → Supervisor
   → Nodal Officer) based on configurable rules or manual triggers.

4. Communication and Notifications

     i.   The system shall provide a secure portal view for complainants to track
       grievance status and view a status history.

     ii.   The system shall send configurable notifications (email/SMS/in-app) on key
       events: registration, assignment, request for additional information, resolution
       proposal, closure, escalation, request for feedback and reopening of ticket.

    iii.   The system shall allow officers to request clarifications from complainants and
       record responses within the same case thread.

  iv.   The system shall maintain a communication log for each grievance, including
        internal notes and external messages, time-stamped and user-stamped.

5. Case Handling and Resolution
     i.   The system shall allow officers to record investigations, actions
       taken, and proposed resolutions against each grievance.

     ii.   The system shall allow attaching and viewing supporting documents and
       evidence (text, images, PDFs, and other file formats as decided) with size
      and type restrictions configurable by AAI Admin.

    iii.   The system shall allow capturing resolution outcome, resolution date, and
        resolution category (e.g., Resolved – Action Taken; Rejected – Insufficient
      Grounds; Referred – Out of Jurisdiction).

  iv.   The system shall allow capturing complainant satisfaction/acceptance (e.g.,
       “Accepted”, “Not Accepted”) prior to final closure where process requires
           it.

   v.   The system shall prevent closure of a grievance unless all mandatory
        resolution fields have been entered.





                               Page 159 of 215

<!-- Page 160 -->

6. Search, Reporting, and Analytics
     i.   The system shall provide search and filtering across grievances by ID,
       complainant, category, date range, status, location, officer, and escalation
        level.

     ii.   The system shall provide standard reports, including at least:

    •  Volume of grievances by category, geography, channel, and time period.

    •  SLA compliance (on-time vs delayed) by category and responsible unit.

    •  Ageing analysis of open grievances.

    iii.   The system shall allow export of report results to CSV/XLSX, subject to role
       permissions.

  iv.   The system shall support configurable dashboards for different roles (e.g.,
      summary tiles, trend charts, SLA alerts).

7. Security, Privacy, and Compliance
     i.   The system shall implement role-based access control so that users see
       only data they are authorized to access.

     ii.   The system shall support strong authentication mechanisms (e.g.,
      SSO, 2FA) as per organization policy.

    iii.   The system shall ensure data in transit, rest and under processing is
       encrypted using HTTPS with current best-practice protocols.

  iv.   The system shall support encryption at rest for sensitive fields as per applicable
        regulations.

   v.   The system shall support masking or restricted access to personally
        identifiable information (PII) for specified roles.

  vi.   The system shall maintain an audit trail of all create/update/delete actions
      on grievances and configuration, with user, timestamp, and action details.

8. Configuration and Administration

     i.   The system shall allow authorized administrators to configure
       grievance categories, subcategories, priority rules, routing rules,
      and SLAs without code changes.

     ii.   The system shall allow configuration of notification templates and
       channels for each event type.

    iii.   The system shall support configuration of working hours, holidays, and
       calendar rules for SLA calculations.

                               Page 160 of 215

<!-- Page 161 -->

             iv.   The system shall provide user and role management (create, deactivate,
              assign roles, reset passwords where not delegated to SSO).

            v.   The system shall allow configuration of multilingual labels and content

         9. Performance, Availability, and Scalability

                    i.   The system shall support concurrent usage by at least 100 internal users
            and 500 external users concurrently, without degradation beyond SLA
               thresholds (2 seconds per transaction maximum).

                    ii.   The system shall load core screens (dashboard, grievance view, grievance
              search) within 2 seconds under normal load.

                   iii.   The system shall meet a minimum uptime as defined in SLA,
              excluding scheduled maintenance windows.

             iv.   The system shall support horizontal or vertical scaling to handle increased
              grievance volumes without major re-architecture.

        10. Usability and Accessibility
                    i.   The system shall follow the MeitY’s GIGW and AAI’s directions for
        UX and provide consistent navigation, labelling, and error handling.

                    ii.   The system shall be accessible and adhere to the chosen accessibility
              standard as per MeitY GIGW standards

                   iii.   The system shall support at least 12 major Indian languages in addition to
              English and Hindi where in scope, with clear switching options.
